/*
  CXMedia.AI — real local demo backend (built 2026-07-22)

  Per direct instruction to wire a real backend + account-record lookup,
  replacing assessment.html's link-carried handoff (base64 payload in
  ?handoff=) with an actual API-backed account record. This is a genuine,
  working server — not a mockup — but it is a LOCAL DEMONSTRATION, not a
  production deployment: it runs in this sandbox only, has no
  authentication, no HTTPS, and its SQLite file has no backup/replication.
  See cxmedia-backend-production-scope.md for what a real production build
  of this same shape requires (auth, hosting, a managed database) before
  this could be exposed to real clients over the internet.

  Built with zero third-party npm packages, on purpose: this sandbox's npm
  registry access returned 403 Forbidden for every package tried (express,
  cors, better-sqlite3) — not a design preference, a hard constraint. So
  this uses only Node 22's built-in http module for the server and its
  built-in (experimental, as of Node 22) node:sqlite module for storage.
  A production build should use whatever real stack is chosen in
  cxmedia-backend-production-scope.md instead of this vanilla-Node shim —
  this is a demonstration of the DATA MODEL and API SHAPE, not a
  recommendation to actually run vanilla Node + node:sqlite in production.

  Schema — deliberately mirrors the shape portal.html's scoreHistory
  already used in memory, just persisted for real now:
    accounts(accountId PK, company, industry, footprint, createdAt)
    score_history(id PK, accountId FK, stage, layer, score, source, recordedAt)
  One row per rating event per cell, same "timestamped snapshot" idea
  Section 6 of cxmedia-client-portal-structure.md describes for a real
  Score History — this IS that table, just running locally for now.

  Added 2026-07-22 (round 2) — Campaign/Project architecture, per
  cxmedia-campaign-project-architecture.md:
    campaigns(id PK, accountId FK, objective, segment, stage, keyMessage, createdAt)
    projects(id PK, campaignId FK, projectType, status, masterContent,
              derivatives [JSON text], createdAt, updatedAt)
  Same zero-npm-package convention as the tables above — no new dependency.
  Every Claude-shaped call that fills masterContent/derivatives is still a
  client-side template (see portal.html's generate*Master()/generate*Derivatives()
  functions) — this backend only persists whatever text the front end sends,
  it does not itself call any AI model.

  Added 2026-07-22 (round 3) — 11-point portal/dashboard redesign:
    campaigns gains: name, budget, plannedImpressions, startDate, endDate,
    isAdHoc — added via a small ensureColumn() migration below so an
    already-existing cxmedia.db file picks up the new columns rather than
    needing a fresh database. isAdHoc marks the one synthetic per-account
    "Ad Hoc Projects" campaign the front end creates lazily so a project can
    be started without a real Campaign (see portal.html's goAdHoc()).

  Added 2026-07-25 (round 59) — CRM integration (Deliverable 13):
    leads(id PK, email, firstName, lastName, phoneCountry, phone, company,
          industry, footprint, buyerType, address, engagementModel,
          quadrant, mediaPct, cxPct, consent, source, hubspotStatus,
          hubspotContactId, hubspotError, createdAt)
  Built against a HubSpot account that doesn't exist yet, per direct
  instruction — every lead from assessment.html's contact step is always
  persisted here first, then synced to HubSpot only if HUBSPOT_ACCESS_TOKEN
  is set as an environment variable. Uses Node 22's built-in global fetch,
  not the HubSpot npm SDK — same zero-third-party-package constraint as the
  rest of this file. See syncLeadToHubspot() below and
  cxmedia-crm-integration-notes.md for the exact setup steps once a HubSpot
  account and Private App token exist — no code change is needed at that
  point, only the env var.

  Added 2026-07-25 (round 60) — real authentication (Deliverable 14). Until
  this round, every account-scoped endpoint trusted whatever accountId
  showed up in the URL — anyone who had or guessed one could read or write
  that account's data, a gap this file's own header comment and
  cxmedia-account-mgmt-scoping.md/cxmedia-backend-production-scope.md had
  already flagged explicitly. This closes it at the level this local demo
  can actually close it at:
    accounts gains: accessCodeHash, accessCodeSalt (scrypt-hashed — see
      hashAccessCode()/verifyAccessCode() below; the plaintext code is
      shown to the client exactly once, at account-creation time, and never
      stored anywhere)
    sessions(token PK, accountId, createdAt, expiresAt)
  Every account-scoped route now calls requireAccount(req, res, accountId)
  before touching the database — see that function and authenticate()
  below. No npm dependency: session tokens use crypto.randomBytes, password
  hashing uses crypto.scryptSync, both built into Node — same
  zero-third-party-package constraint as everything else in this file.
  This is a genuine, working auth layer, not a mock — but it's still a
  hand-rolled one running over plain HTTP with no HTTPS, and per
  cxmedia-account-mgmt-scoping.md Section 1, a real production build should
  replace it with a real identity provider (that doc suggests Supabase
  Auth) rather than extend this further. This closes the local-demo gap
  using the same "genuinely real, deliberately not production-grade"
  posture the rest of this backend already takes.
*/

const http = require('http');
const path = require('path');
const crypto = require('crypto');
const fs = require('fs');
const { spawn } = require('child_process');

// Database: local SQLite file when running standalone (unchanged local-dev
// behavior), or Supabase Postgres when DATABASE_URL is set (production /
// Vercel). Both expose the same synchronous db.exec/db.prepare(...).get|all|run
// interface, so every query call site below works unmodified either way.
// See pg-sync-bridge.js for how the Postgres path stays synchronous.
const DB_PATH = path.join(__dirname, 'cxmedia.db');
let db;
if (process.env.DATABASE_URL) {
  const { createSyncDb } = require('./pg-sync-bridge');
  db = createSyncDb(process.env.DATABASE_URL);
  console.log('CXMedia.AI backend: using Supabase/Postgres (DATABASE_URL set)');
} else {
  const { DatabaseSync } = require('node:sqlite');
  db = new DatabaseSync(DB_PATH);
  console.log('CXMedia.AI backend: using local SQLite file (no DATABASE_URL set)');
}

db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS accounts (
    accountId TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    company TEXT NOT NULL,
    industry TEXT,
    footprint TEXT,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL
  );
`);
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS score_history (
    id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    stage TEXT NOT NULL,
    layer TEXT NOT NULL,
    score REAL NOT NULL,
    source TEXT NOT NULL,
    recordedAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId)
  );
`);
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS campaigns (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    objective TEXT NOT NULL,
    segment TEXT,
    stage TEXT,
    keyMessage TEXT,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId)
  );
`);
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS projects (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    campaignId TEXT NOT NULL,
    projectType TEXT NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'draft',
    masterContent TEXT,
    derivatives TEXT,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    updatedAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (campaignId) REFERENCES campaigns(id)
  );
`);

// Lightweight migration (added 2026-07-22, round 3) — the Active Campaign
// view + Ad Hoc project path (per the 11-point portal redesign) need new
// columns on an already-existing `campaigns` table. node:sqlite has no
// "ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS", so this just attempts each ALTER TABLE and
// swallows the "duplicate column" error on any re-run/existing db file.
function ensureColumn(table, col, decl){
  try { db.exec(`ALTER TABLE ${table} ADD COLUMN ${col} ${decl}`); }
  catch (e) { /* column already exists — fine */ }
}
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'name', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'budget', 'REAL');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'plannedImpressions', 'INTEGER');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'startDate', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'endDate', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'isAdHoc', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
// Round 132av (2026-08-12) — the new cross-campaign Creative handoff grid's
// two status checkboxes (Active/Complete). Same merge-update convention as
// every other campaign field below; default Active=1 so every existing
// campaign shows as active rather than silently defaulting to inactive.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'creativeActive', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 1');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'creativeComplete', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
// Round 132av — real landing page audit (content/imagery relevance + CTA
// path performance), cached so the QA panel doesn't need to re-fetch the
// live page on every render; only refreshed when the "Run landing page
// audit" button fires.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'landingPageAuditJson', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'landingPageAuditAt', 'TEXT');

// Round 132[aov-poc] (2026-08-17) — Business Outcome Relevance (AI-scored
// Brand Messaging copy) and AI-generated Long Form Copy, both cached the
// same way the landing-page audit above is: only refreshed when their own
// explicit "Score with AI"/"Generate with AI" action fires, not on every
// render.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'messagingRelevanceJson', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'messagingRelevanceScoredAt', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-07-22 (round 16) — campaign lifecycle status bar + MVP manual
// Analysis inputs, per direct instruction. `status` is one of
// portal.html's CAMPAIGN_LIFECYCLE_STEPS ('Objective & Details' | 'In
// Market' | 'Analysis'); the DEFAULT here means every existing row and
// every future INSERT that doesn't specify the column starts on step 1
// automatically. The four actual* / analysisNotes columns are the "manual
// inputs for the MVP" on the final (Analysis) step — there's no live
// analytics feed in this build, so these are typed in by hand.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'status', "TEXT DEFAULT 'Objective & Details'");
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'actualSpend', 'REAL');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'actualImpressions', 'INTEGER');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'actualConversions', 'INTEGER');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'analysisNotes', 'TEXT');
// Round 89 — actualReach joins actualSpend/actualImpressions per direct
// instruction to align campaign creation with the MMM taxonomy: campaigns
// now report actuals per MMM category (see campaign_mmm_line_items below),
// and actualSpend/actualReach/actualImpressions become computed sums of
// those line items rather than three separately hand-typed numbers. See the
// campaign_mmm_line_items comment for the full design.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'actualReach', 'INTEGER');

// Added 2026-07-22 (round 17) — Marketing Functions selected at campaign
// creation, so Campaign Detail can show which functions were "activated"
// for this campaign, not just its stage. Comma-joined string, same shape
// this build already uses for `segment`, rather than a join table.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'functions', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-07-23 (round 31) — Campaign URL & Conversion Tracking, per
// direct instruction: an optional landing-page URL for the campaign, plus
// the conversion KPI type it's meant to drive (same fixed vocabulary as
// portal.html's CONVERSION_TYPES — eCommerce/Call Center/Storefront), so
// the client-side URL Audit (pure URL-structure/UTM-parameter parsing, no
// live fetch — this build has no server-side crawling) has a persisted
// conversionType to check the URL against instead of an ephemeral,
// page-local selection.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'campaignUrl', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'conversionType', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-07-24 (round 36) — the 7-stage campaign progress bar. Most
// stages auto-derive from existing signals (project approval/QA, `status`,
// the actual*/analysisNotes columns above), but Brand and QA & Approval each
// need one new manually-set field: brandStage (Not Started/In Progress/
// Complete — no live per-campaign brand-asset signal exists yet) and
// qaApproved (set once by a team member after Marketing Functions + Creative
// are both Complete).
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'brandStage', "TEXT DEFAULT 'Not Started'");
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'qaApproved', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');

// Added 2026-07-27 (round 65) — Brand card: campaign-level brand messaging,
// distinct from the account-level Brand Voice/Style Assets/Competitive
// Positioning screens (those already have their own columns on `accounts`,
// below). keyMessage already existed (set once at creation) but was never
// updatable after the fact — folded into the same merge-update endpoint as
// these two new fields so the Brand card can edit all three together.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'brandToneNotes', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'brandGuidelines', 'TEXT');
// The Creative Brief and Longform Copy every creative asset's copy
// recommendation is derived from client-side (see portal.html's
// recommendCopyForJob()), based on each Creative Job's own spec.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'creativeBrief', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'longformCopy', 'TEXT');

// Round 73 — Ad Trafficking send. trafficPackageJson is a snapshot (jobId,
// channel, spec, trackingUrl, assetApprovedAt per job) taken at the moment
// of sending — not a live query — so what the trafficking team received
// stays visible even if a job's data changes afterward.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'trafficSentAt', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'trafficPackageJson', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-08-05 (round 32, part C) — elevated Objective & Success
// Metrics, per direct instruction: a curated Primary KPI, a numeric KPI
// Goal (its unit is whatever Primary KPI names — leads, transactions,
// etc.), and a Transaction Window in days (defaults from the Objective's
// inferred Media Loop stage — see portal.html's
// TRANSACTION_WINDOW_DAYS_BY_STAGE/OBJECTIVE_PRIMARY_STAGE — editable by a
// human regardless of the default).
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'primaryKpi', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'kpiGoal', 'INTEGER');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'transactionWindowDays', 'INTEGER');
// Added 2026-08-05 (round 32, part D) — recommended channel mix, audience
// mix, and channel spend allocation, stored as one JSON blob (same
// convention as trafficPackageJson above): { channels, audience, source,
// updatedAt }. Computed client-side at creation and whenever a human edits
// it — this column is just the persisted snapshot, not computed here.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'mediaMixJson', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-08-06 (round 99) — Performance Marketing's validation + audience
// handoff to Omni-Channel Creative, per direct instruction to restructure the
// Portal's "What do you want to do" around roles (Manage Budget, Brand,
// Marketing Ops, Performance Marketing, Omni-Channel Creative, PR, Analysis).
// audienceTargets is the single shared field Performance Marketing writes
// once it validates the recommended channel budgets/buys/programmatic
// allocations — Omni-Channel Creative reads this same field, read-only, per
// direct decision that this should be one shared campaign-level field rather
// than a separate handoff object. pmValidatedAt records when Performance
// Marketing last confirmed the recommended mix (null/unset = not yet
// validated).
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'audienceTargets', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'pmValidatedAt', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-08-10 (round 132w), per direct instruction — Product Group /
// Creative Focus Group, populated from the real Marketing Calendar demo
// data. These have been collected on the Campaign Creation form since
// round 132c and portal.html's cmpBuildFormPayload() has been sending them
// in the create POST body ever since — but this table never had matching
// columns, so the backend silently dropped them on every insert and every
// campaign fetched back from here came back with them blank, regardless
// of what was picked at creation. Comma-joined strings, same convention as
// segment/functions/channels. See backfill-product-creative-groups.js for
// the one-time backfill this unblocks for the 92 pre-existing AOV-seeded
// campaigns (seeded before this column existed, so they have no
// campaign-level value of their own — backfilled from their real
// channel_planning_details rows instead, which have always carried
// productGroup/creativeMarket per line item).
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'productGroups', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'creativeFocusGroups', 'TEXT');

// Round 132aa (2026-08-10) — Performance Marketing asset-selection rework,
// per direct instruction: "Performance Marketing selects the assets based
// on the plan buy types and partners selected... Once selected and
// approved they are then sent to Brand... Only the assets designated by
// brand will be available at the campaign level." approvedAssetJobIds is
// the comma-joined set of this campaign's creative_jobs.id values
// Performance Marketing has designated in scope — every downstream stage
// (Brand, Creative accordion, Trafficking) filters to this set once it's
// non-empty; an empty/unset value is an honest fallback for campaigns that
// predate this gate, or haven't reached this step yet, and shows every
// creative job as before. pmAssetsApprovedAt timestamps the approval so
// the UI can show "last approved" the same way pmValidatedAt already does
// for the audience-targets validation this sits alongside.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'approvedAssetJobIds', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'pmAssetsApprovedAt', 'TEXT');
// Round 132bb (2026-08-10) — human-readable companion to
// approvedAssetJobIds (channel/deliveryPlatform labels, comma-joined),
// saved alongside it so generateCreativeBrief() can reference which assets
// Performance Marketing actually approved without a second async fetch of
// the opaque job IDs at brief-render time.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'approvedAssetSummary', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-07-23 (round 18) — Brand Foundations, Voice component. Per
// cxmedia-brand-foundations-plan.md: Voice was previously local-only in
// portal.html (state.voice, reset on every reload), which can't meaningfully
// gate anything since the "approved" flag would never survive a page
// refresh. Persisting it here is what makes gating Campaign/Ad Hoc creation
// on Voice approval workable. 1:1 with an account, same as company/
// industry/footprint — extends `accounts`, not a new table.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'voiceGuideText', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'voiceApproved', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'voiceVersion', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'voiceApprovedAt', 'TEXT');
// 2026-08-22 — the multi-model Brand Voice contest, per direct instruction:
// "add a multi-model contest in the brand setup section that presents an
// overall voice theme to include a vision statement and longform copy
// example that incorporates the critical customer facing messages." Kept
// separate from voiceGuideText (the existing single-draft field) rather than
// folded into it — a Vision Statement and a Longform Example are each their
// own addressable thing, not fragments of one blob. See
// runBrandVoiceContest() below for how these get filled in.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'visionStatement', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'longformVoiceExample', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-07-23 (round 19) — Company Profile (Brand Foundations). The
// free assessment already collects target audience (generations) and
// wealth tier alongside industry/footprint (assessment.html's Step 1), but
// audience/wealth were never actually sent to this backend or persisted —
// only company/industry/footprint were. Comma-joined strings, same shape
// already used for `segment`/`functions` on campaigns, rather than a join
// table — this is a short, fixed-vocabulary multi-select on both fields.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'audience', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'wealth', 'TEXT');
// Added 2026-08-05 (round 32, follow-on) — which of the 18 MMM_CATEGORIES
// channels this account has actually activated. Comma-joined, same shape
// as audience/wealth above. Empty/null means "never configured" — the
// front end defaults to everything active rather than treating that as
// nothing active.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'activeChannels', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-07-23 (round 20) — Style Guide: Colors & Fonts (Brand
// Foundations). Per cxmedia-brand-foundations-plan.md Section 3: this is
// reference-asset storage, not a generative task, so these are structured
// reference fields (a hex code, a font family name, a filename reference)
// rather than a generated draft like Voice. `styleSourceFileName` records
// an uploaded document's name only — this prototype has no real file
// storage (no auth/HTTPS/hosting, see cxmedia-backend-production-scope.md),
// so the actual bytes never leave the browser; the team member copies the
// key details into the structured fields below instead.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'styleAssetsApproved', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'logoUrl', 'TEXT');
// Round 130 (2026-08-08), per direct instruction — real logo file upload,
// stored as a base64 data URI (see portal.html's handleLogoFileUpload()
// comment for why: no separate blob/file storage service exists in this
// prototype). logoUrl above is kept as an optional link-only fallback.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'logoDataUrl', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'logoFileName', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'primaryColorName', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'primaryColorHex', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'secondaryPaletteText', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'headingFontName', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'headingFontNotes', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'bodyFontName', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'bodyFontNotes', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'styleNotes', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'styleSourceFileName', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'styleAssetsApprovedAt', 'TEXT');
// Round 33 — best-practice gap closed against a real uploaded brand
// guideline (Atlas Ocean Voyages Mini Brand Guidelines v1.0): logo
// colorway/exclusion-zone/misuse rules, color weighting/hierarchy, and a
// typography hierarchy (H1-H4/body/caption) weren't captured anywhere
// before, even though every real brand guide we've seen specifies them.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'logoUsageNotes', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'colorHierarchyNotes', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'typographyHierarchyNotes', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-07-23 (round 30) — Team & Org Chart, per direct instruction:
// a modern marketing org chart with real hierarchy (CMO → Director →
// Manager → Specialist), so team members can be added with a role and a
// reporting line, and the Portal can resolve "my direct manager" / "my
// boss's boss" for a QA request. A real table (not an accounts column)
// since an account can have many team members — same shape as campaigns/
// projects above. reportsToId is a self-referencing FK (nullable — the CMO
// reports to no one in this account's chart).
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS team_members (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    name TEXT NOT NULL,
    functionGroup TEXT NOT NULL,
    level TEXT NOT NULL,
    reportsToId TEXT,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId)
  );
`);

// Added 2026-07-25 (round 43) — reconciling account.html's Team Members
// section with the Team & Org Chart's roster instead of the two staying
// two separate, disconnected role vocabularies (account.html previously
// had its own flat Admin/Brand/Planner/Voice/Analyst/AI-Ops list, entirely
// local/mocked, never read by the QA-flow org chart). Per direct
// instruction: this table is now the single source of truth for team
// membership, and "Admin" (full account/billing access) is an orthogonal
// flag on top of a person's org-chart Level/Function — an Admin isn't
// automatically the CMO, and a Specialist isn't automatically locked out
// of being an Admin. email/status didn't exist on this table before
// (account.html tracked them locally) — added here so invites and
// active/inactive status also persist for real instead of resetting.
ensureColumn('team_members', 'email', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('team_members', 'isAdmin', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
ensureColumn('team_members', 'status', "TEXT DEFAULT 'active'");

// Added 2026-08-18 — real per-person username/password auth (registration
// rebuild), per direct instruction: individually memorable credentials
// instead of the one-per-account access code. passwordHash/passwordSalt
// use the same scrypt hashing as accessCodeHash/Salt (see
// hashAccessCode()/verifyAccessCode() below — generic despite the name,
// reused here rather than duplicating the KDF call). mustChangePassword
// forces a real password to be set on first login after a system-issued
// temp password. phone/phoneVerifiedAt are schema-only for now — SMS
// verification itself is deferred (no provider account set up yet); the
// columns exist so that piece can slot in later without another
// migration.
ensureColumn('team_members', 'passwordHash', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('team_members', 'passwordSalt', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('team_members', 'mustChangePassword', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
ensureColumn('team_members', 'phone', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('team_members', 'phoneVerifiedAt', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-08-19 — paid tier/level plumbing (auth-path Path 4). Per
// direct instruction: final tier names/pricing/inclusions are TBD — this
// only stores WHICH of the placeholder tiers (see PAID_TIERS below) an
// account picked and when, so the login-facing plumbing (select a tier →
// land on the account page for it) is real even though the tiers
// themselves aren't finalized yet. Nullable — an account with no tier
// selected is the existing free/unassigned state, unchanged.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'paidTier', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'paidTierActivatedAt', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-08-19 — placeholder paid tiers, per direct instruction: final
// names/pricing/inclusions are still TBD, but the login-facing plumbing
// (select a tier -> land on the account page for it, either by creating a
// new account or activating it on an existing one) needs real values to
// validate against today. `key` is the only thing actually stored
// anywhere (accounts.paidTier) — name/description are display-only and
// meant to be trivially swappable once real tiers are decided; nothing
// downstream keys off the display text.
const PAID_TIERS = [
  { key: 'tier-1', name: 'Level 1 (name TBD)', description: 'Placeholder — final scope and pricing not yet decided.' },
  { key: 'tier-2', name: 'Level 2 (name TBD)', description: 'Placeholder — final scope and pricing not yet decided.' },
  { key: 'tier-3', name: 'Level 3 (name TBD)', description: 'Placeholder — final scope and pricing not yet decided.' }
];

// Added 2026-08-19 — one row per phone-verification attempt (recovery
// flow and, later, the still-deferred registration anti-bot control).
// Real Twilio Verify integration reads/writes this once TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID/
// TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN/TWILIO_VERIFY_SERVICE_SID are set (see sendVerificationCode()/
// checkVerificationCode() below); until then, requestCode is generated
// locally and returned once in the API response (interim on-screen code,
// per direct instruction) instead of actually being texted anywhere.
db.exec(`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS phone_verifications (
  id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  memberId TEXT,
  phone TEXT NOT NULL,
  purpose TEXT NOT NULL,
  codeHash TEXT,
  codeSalt TEXT,
  provider TEXT NOT NULL,
  verified INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
  attempts INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
  createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
  expiresAt TEXT NOT NULL
)`);
// 2026-08-20 — signup-verification (anti-bot) build. `channel` lets this
// same table carry email-target rows too, not just phone: the `phone`
// column holds whichever target address the code was sent to (a real
// E.164 phone for channel='sms', an email address for channel='email') —
// per direct instruction ("email is also acceptable if we run into
// issues [with phone]"), so a visitor whose carrier blocks Twilio SMS, or
// who simply prefers it, isn't locked out of creating an account.
// `consumedAt` is what stops one verified code from provisioning more
// than one account — see POST /api/auth/register-admin below, which is
// the actual gate now that "actual accounts can't be created without
// phone verification" is a hard requirement, not just recovery-flow
// plumbing.
ensureColumn('phone_verifications', 'channel', "TEXT DEFAULT 'sms'");
ensureColumn('phone_verifications', 'consumedAt', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-08-19 — password-reset tokens. One-time-use, short-lived,
// looked up by a random token (never the memberId itself, so a guessed
// or leaked memberId alone can't be used to reset a password). Requires
// a verified phone_verifications row for the same memberId within the
// last 15 minutes before the reset itself is allowed to complete — see
// POST /api/auth/reset-password.
db.exec(`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS password_resets (
  token TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  memberId TEXT NOT NULL,
  createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
  expiresAt TEXT NOT NULL,
  usedAt TEXT
)`);

// Added 2026-08-20 — login step-up MFA ("new device" verification), per
// direct instruction after discussing that budgets/strategy data behind
// login deserve more than password-only protection. Design: password-only
// login stays instant on a device that's already proven itself once;
// a device signing in for the first time (or after its trust expires)
// must also enter a 6-digit code sent to the member's phone (falling back
// to email if no phone is on file — every member always has an email, so
// there's always a channel, unlike request-password-reset's silent no-op
// when a phone is missing). See POST /api/auth/login-user,
// /request-login-mfa, and /verify-login-code below. This table holds the
// resulting long-lived device tokens — 30 days, distinct from the 7-day
// session token in `sessions` above, and only ever created by a
// successful /verify-login-code call. The token itself is opaque and
// random (same convention as `sessions.token`), stored by the frontend in
// localStorage (not sessionStorage — it must outlive the tab/session by
// design, a deliberate, narrow exception to this app's usual
// sessionStorage-for-security-tokens convention) keyed by the login
// email, so it can be sent back on the next login attempt from the same
// browser before a session even exists.
db.exec(`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS trusted_devices (
  token TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  memberId TEXT NOT NULL,
  createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
  expiresAt TEXT NOT NULL,
  lastUsedAt TEXT
)`);

// Added 2026-07-25 (round 46) — HEO function 1 of 3, self-rating. One row
// per skill per rating event (not one row per member) so a trend over time
// is just "every row for this memberId+skill, ordered by ratedAt" — same
// append-only shape as score_history above, not an update-in-place. `skill`
// stores the literal skill text from portal.html's SKILL_SETS (round 45)
// rather than a foreign key into some skills table, since SKILL_SETS is a
// static in-file object, not a backend-owned resource — same
// hand-kept-in-sync convention already used for RUBRIC_CELLS/FUNCTION_GROUPS
// elsewhere in this build.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS self_ratings (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    memberId TEXT NOT NULL,
    skill TEXT NOT NULL,
    score REAL NOT NULL,
    source TEXT NOT NULL,
    ratedAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (memberId) REFERENCES team_members(id)
  );
`);

// Added 2026-08-18 — legal acceptance audit trail (registration rebuild),
// per direct instruction: every instance of legal acceptance (standard
// Terms & Conditions / pricing disclosures at registration, and NDA if a
// client opts into one) gets persisted with a timestamp and identifying
// user info, regardless of which document(s) were involved. No signature
// is required for the standard registration documents — completing
// registration itself is the acceptance (acceptanceMethod
// 'assumed-on-save'); an NDA, if signed, is logged the same way but with
// acceptanceMethod 'signature' since that one's still a deliberate opt-in
// action. documentVersion exists so a future change to the T&Cs/pricing
// text doesn't retroactively blur what an existing client actually agreed
// to — this is provisional (assumed-on-save) pending a formal legal
// opinion, per direct instruction, so acceptanceMethod is what lets that
// change later without a schema migration.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS legal_acceptances (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    memberId TEXT,
    documentType TEXT NOT NULL,
    documentVersion TEXT NOT NULL,
    acceptedAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    acceptedByName TEXT,
    acceptedByEmail TEXT,
    ipAddress TEXT,
    userAgent TEXT,
    acceptanceMethod TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId)
  );
`);

// Added 2026-07-23 (round 22) — Competitive Positioning (Brand Foundations),
// the third and final gate component per cxmedia-brand-foundations-plan.md.
// Industry Trends is a generated draft (prompt-ready, same shape as Voice —
// one text field). Direct Competitors is up to 5 team-entered companies
// within the account's own industry, each optionally paired with an
// uploaded reference document (filename-only, same honest non-parsing
// convention as Style Assets) and a generated positioning read (hybrid —
// reasoning about *why* the competitor competes for the same customer,
// never asserting unverified facts about what that competitor is actually
// doing). Stored as a single JSON array (`competitorsJson`) rather than a
// join table — a small, fixed-size (<=5) list that's always read/written
// as a whole alongside the rest of this account's Brand Foundations data.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'competitivePositioningApproved', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'competitivePositioningApprovedAt', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'industryTrendsText', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'competitorsJson', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-07-23 (round 32) — Search Everywhere (SEO/AEO/GEO) keyword
// recommendations, per direct instruction. A single JSON array, same
// "small, always read/written as a whole" convention as competitorsJson
// above — each entry is {keyword, volumeBand, rationale}, generated
// client-side from the account's own profile + approved Brand Voice (see
// portal.html's generateSearchKeywords()) and editable (remove any entry)
// before saving.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'searchKeywordsJson', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-08-05 (round 32, follow-on) — Products & Services (short free
// text naming what this brand actually sells) and Brand & Product Keywords
// (a JSON object { brand: [], product: [], negative: [] } of human-editable
// keyword chips, drafted from Brand Voice's tone anchors + Products &
// Services rather than Industry — see portal.html's
// generateBrandProductKeywords()). Search Everywhere's own keyword
// generator was rebuilt to draw from this instead of Industry too, per
// direct instruction not to use Industry as the basis for either.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'productsServices', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'brandKeywordsJson', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-08-05 (round 33) — the assessment's Company URL and brand
// description, per direct instruction to bring over all of the
// assessment's data rather than dropping it before the backend handoff
// (assessment.html captured both in state.url/state.description but never
// sent them). Read-only provenance context on Company Profile — not
// re-editable structured fields the way Industry/Footprint are, since
// there's nowhere else for a prose paragraph to write back to.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'websiteUrl', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'assessmentDescription', 'TEXT');
// Round 132bg (2026-08-13) — durable brand context from POST
// /api/accounts/:id/website-scan (see that endpoint's own comment). Stored
// on the account, not per-campaign, since it's real brand-level content
// (title/meta description/headings from this account's own real site), the
// same tier as the Voice Guide/Style Notes below.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'websiteContextJson', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'websiteContextFetchedAt', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-07-23 (round 34) — which Media Loop stages the intake
// assessment actually picked (assessment.html Step 3 / state.assessStages),
// sent once at account creation and never changed after. A JSON array of
// stage names (e.g. ["Awareness","Purchase"]). Lets the Portal's quadrant
// flag a stage the visitor never rated instead of silently scoring it —
// see assessment.html's computeScores() and portal.html's
// computeQuadrantScores() for the math this feeds. Null on any account
// created before this round; both files treat null as "every stage
// assessed" so nothing already scored changes.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'assessedStagesJson', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-07-25 (round 53) — Media Plan & Budget architecture, per
// cxmedia-media-plan-budget-scope.md. media_plans is one row per account per
// year; media_plan_allocations holds the two-tier (Media Loop stage, then
// channel) split, recommended vs. approved amounts kept as separate columns
// so the mocked recommendation and the human-adjusted final number are both
// visible, never silently overwritten. Campaigns gain a nullable
// mediaPlanId/allocationId (null = unplanned, running outside the plan by
// design, not an error state) and demandSignalRef for the Continuous
// Monitoring finding a campaign responds to, when there is one.
// Simplification for this prototype pass, per the scoping doc: a campaign
// draws against exactly one allocation, stored directly on the campaigns row
// (fundingSource/allocationId/budget), rather than the fuller
// campaign_allocation_draws join table the doc sketches for a campaign that
// spans several channels — same "one flat field now, normalize later"
// shortcut this build already used for segment/functions.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS media_plans (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    year INTEGER NOT NULL,
    totalBudget REAL,
    usesTranches INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    updatedAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId)
  );
`);
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS media_plan_allocations (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    mediaPlanId TEXT NOT NULL,
    stage TEXT NOT NULL,
    channel TEXT NOT NULL,
    recommendedAmount REAL,
    approvedAmount REAL,
    FOREIGN KEY (mediaPlanId) REFERENCES media_plans(id)
  );
`);
// Added 2026-07-25 (round 54) — tranche period-level breakdown. One row per
// (allocation, period label) — e.g. an allocation's annual approvedAmount
// split into Q1–Q4 or Jan–Dec rows. Only meaningful when the parent plan's
// usesTranches is on; the front end doesn't render/save these when it's
// off, but the table has no such constraint itself (an account that
// switches tranches off keeps its period rows on file rather than losing
// them, in case it switches back on).
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS media_plan_periods (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    allocationId TEXT NOT NULL,
    label TEXT NOT NULL,
    approvedAmount REAL,
    FOREIGN KEY (allocationId) REFERENCES media_plan_allocations(id)
  );
`);
ensureColumn('media_plans', 'trancheGranularity', "TEXT DEFAULT 'quarterly'");
// Round 132bx follow-on (2026-08-15), per direct bug report: the frontend's
// Save Plan action (saveMediaPlan()) has sent grossRevenue, nonWorkingMedia,
// and the plan-wide monthlyMode/monthlyPct curve in its POST body since
// those features shipped (rounds 108, 116, 117) — this table just never had
// columns for them, so the backend silently accepted and dropped all four
// on every save. Confirmed live: Non-Working Media rows (including ones
// seeded by the new "Prefill from confirmed upload" button) vanished on
// reload even though "Saved — saved to the backend" reported success. Fixed
// by adding the missing columns and wiring both endpoints below to actually
// read/write them, same JSON-column convention this file already uses for
// other small structured fields (contentLibraryJson, competitorsJson, etc.)
// — nonWorkingMedia and monthlyPct are both arbitrary-shaped, so stored as
// JSON text rather than normalized tables, matching that precedent rather
// than inventing new child tables for a prototype-stage feature.
ensureColumn('media_plans', 'grossRevenue', 'REAL');
ensureColumn('media_plans', 'nonWorkingMediaJson', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('media_plans', 'monthlyMode', "TEXT DEFAULT 'flat'");
ensureColumn('media_plans', 'monthlyPctJson', 'TEXT');
// Round 132c13 (2026-08-17), per direct instruction — the "apply a
// percentage of revenue" calculator on the frontend (Media Plan's option
// C). marketingBudgetPct/tradeMarketingPct are the two % figures the user
// last applied (kept so re-opening this plan/year shows what was used, not
// re-applied automatically — see mpApplyRevenuePct() on the frontend).
// tradeMarketingBudget is its own dollar figure, deliberately NOT the same
// column as totalBudget — Sales & Trade Marketing is a separate budget
// line from the working-media Annual Marketing Budget the channel
// allocations draw from, same distinction Non-Working Media already draws
// for agency/analytics/production spend.
ensureColumn('media_plans', 'marketingBudgetPct', 'REAL');
ensureColumn('media_plans', 'tradeMarketingPct', 'REAL');
ensureColumn('media_plans', 'tradeMarketingBudget', 'REAL');
// Added 2026-08-05 — per-allocation estimated CPM (round 32C follow-on).
// Editable per stage+channel row rather than one rate per channel account-
// wide, since the same channel can realistically cost differently by
// funnel stage (e.g. Direct Mail at Awareness vs. Loyalty). Feeds the
// grid's estimated-impressions readout and, once a campaign draws against
// this allocation, that campaign's own Planned Impressions estimate.
ensureColumn('media_plan_allocations', 'cpm', 'REAL');
// Added 2026-08-05 (round 32, follow-on) — optional Audience Type tag
// (blended/past/new) per allocation row, per direct instruction on the
// Loop concept: audiences move in and out of every funnel stage, so
// budgeting by audience is sometimes what actually matters, not just
// stage. Additive — defaults to 'blended', existing allocations and the
// campaign-draw-by-stage flow are unaffected until a plan owner opts a
// specific row into a split via "Split by audience."
ensureColumn('media_plan_allocations', 'audienceType', "TEXT DEFAULT 'blended'");
// Round 132c[8] (2026-08-16) — buildout item #8, "per-allocation stage
// playbook field (audience, creative direction, KPI notes)." Free-text,
// optional, per (stage, channel) row — this is execution guidance on top
// of a mix the account is already running, not something that changes what
// gets recommended or how it's measured, so it's plain text rather than a
// structured field with its own validation.
ensureColumn('media_plan_allocations', 'playbookAudience', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('media_plan_allocations', 'playbookCreative', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('media_plan_allocations', 'playbookKpi', 'TEXT');
// A per-allocation Remarketing tag briefly lived here (round 32, follow-on)
// but per direct feedback didn't flow anywhere useful at the Media Plan
// level — remarketing is a campaign-level decision, not a plan-level one,
// so it'll be built at Campaign Creation/Detail instead. The
// media_plan_allocations.remarketing column may still exist on databases
// that ran that migration; it's simply unused now, left alone rather than
// risk a destructive column drop.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'channels', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'fundingSource', "TEXT DEFAULT 'unplanned'");
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'allocationId', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'demandSignalRef', 'TEXT');

// Round 132be (2026-08-12) — "Flag this style for future editorial
// improvements" (formerly "Save final version for AI Agent Training"),
// per direct correction: "we're saving style for future editorial
// improvements. The intent is not to save completed work unless the user
// copies an existing campaign." Real backend columns for the first time —
// the prior version's frontend claimed "saved to the backend" on a
// successful POST, but messagingTrainingExample was never actually part
// of that endpoint's merge/UPDATE, so nothing was truly persisted
// server-side despite the honest-sounding note. Fixed here alongside the
// scope change: messagingStyleDigestJson holds only structural signal
// (Role/Style approach, Message Type, sentence-length/paragraph shape) —
// never the literal Key Message/Long Form Copy text — per portal.html's
// cmpMessagingStyleDigest().
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'messagingTrainingExample', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'messagingTrainingExampleAt', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'messagingStyleDigestJson', 'TEXT');
// Round 132bx (2026-08-13) — new Campaign header's Active/Pending/
// Completed/Cancelled status. The first 3 are pure date math off
// startDate/endDate (see portal.html's cmpLifecycleStatus()) and need no
// storage of their own; Cancelled is the one state that's a manual,
// date-independent action, so it needs a real column — a simple boolean
// flag plus a timestamp for "when," same minimal-shape convention as
// qaApproved/creativeActive above. Per direct instruction, cancelling a
// campaign removes its budget from every rollup that sums campaign
// budgets (Media Plan committed total, Budget Mix) — no cancellation-fee
// logic, a cancelled campaign's budget is simply treated as $0 in those
// sums (see portal.html's cmpEffectiveBudgetForRollup()).
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'cancelled', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'cancelledAt', 'TEXT');

// Round 132c17 (2026-08-17), per direct instruction: "the campaign id
// includes a 3 letter code for each partner defined during setup, three
// letter product code -last two of start year -last two of month year."
// This is a NEW, human-readable Campaign Code — the existing `id` column
// (opaque CMP-... string) stays the real primary/foreign key everywhere it's
// already wired (creative_jobs, channel_planning_details, campaign_mmm_line_
// items, etc. — ~230 references), so nothing about existing joins changes.
// campaignCode is generated at creation (see generateCampaignCode() below)
// from the account's partnerCode (Company Profile) + this campaign's own
// productCode/productName + its startDate — and is what's shown to users
// and used to key non-working-media expense rows and Direct Mail postage.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'partnerCode', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'productCode', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'productName', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'campaignCode', 'TEXT');

// 2026-08-22 — two of the three "next copywriting priorities" picked up
// after closing out the original CX & Copywriting punch list.
//   roleStyle       — NOT a new selector. portal.html already has a real,
//                      built Role/Style system (LONGFORM_ROLE_STYLE,
//                      #cmpLongformStyle — Storytelling/Tactical
//                      Offer/Brand Promise/Discovery/Launch/Brand Campaign)
//                      that drives the deterministic Long Form Copy
//                      generator — but it lives only in the DOM select's
//                      live value, never saved, so it resets on reload and
//                      the real-AI generator (generateMessagingCopyViaAI/
//                      generateInterviewCandidateCopy below) never sees it
//                      unless the specific caller that fired the request
//                      happened to read the select itself. This column is
//                      the missing persistence for that already-real
//                      concept, and a fallback source for callers (like the
//                      Interview panel) that don't already thread styleKey
//                      through themselves.
//   keyMessageMode  — this one IS new. Distinct from the existing Message
//                      Type field (#cmpKeyMessageType/LONGFORM_ROLE_STYLE's
//                      sibling axis — Offer/Launch/Brand Campaign, which
//                      shapes tone/register) — keyMessageMode instead
//                      governs whether Key Message itself must survive
//                      verbatim. 'exact' (default, unchanged behavior — Key
//                      Message is real human-authored copy that belongs
//                      verbatim) or 'descriptive' (a directional brief
//                      instead of a sentence that must reproduce
//                      word-for-word). See validateOutputAgainstObjective()'s
//                      "Key Message reflected" check in portal.html for how
//                      this changes what "reflected" means for a
//                      descriptive-mode brief.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'roleStyle', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'keyMessageMode', "TEXT DEFAULT 'exact'");

// 2026-08-22 -- messageType and mandatoryPhrase are the two fields the
// roleStyle/keyMessageMode comment above explicitly named as still
// local-session-only (cmpPersistMessagingToCampaign(), portal.html: "no
// backend column yet, same honest disclosure as briefBuilderExpandedText").
// That was a real, live gap, not just a documentation note: the Marketing
// Function Project generators built over the last 2 rounds
// (generateSocialBatchMaster/generateEmailSequenceMaster/etc., and their
// Press Release/Internal Comms/One-Pager counterparts) now compose their
// CTAs from campaign.messageType (via MESSAGE_TYPE_CUE) -- so on any
// browser reload, a saved campaign's Message Type reverted to unset and
// that CTA composition silently stopped reflecting it. mandatoryPhrase
// (real, human-authored required text -- e.g. "50% Off. Promotion Expires
// July 31." -- distinct in kind from every other field, per round 132ad's
// own decision to treat it as verbatim-required rather than a directional
// signal) had the exact same gap. Both now persist the same way
// roleStyle/keyMessageMode already do.
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'messageType', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('campaigns', 'mandatoryPhrase', 'TEXT');

// Added 2026-07-25 (round 53) — org_model (team/solo), per the Media Plan
// scoping doc's solo/local-business flow folded in as a parallel track, not
// a separate build. Governs UI density in the front end (Team & Org Chart,
// HEO features, Media Plan tranche complexity) — this backend just persists
// the flag, same "front end decides what to render" pattern as everywhere
// else.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'orgModel', "TEXT DEFAULT 'team'");

// Added 2026-07-25 (round 55) — content library (saved boilerplate, legal
// disclaimer language, testimonial bank), per the campaign audit's "no
// reuse/template mechanism anywhere" finding. Small JSON blob, same
// always-read/written-as-a-whole convention as competitorsJson/
// searchKeywordsJson above.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'contentLibraryJson', 'TEXT');
// Round 55 — structured content fields for Press Release / Social Content
// Batch (headline, dateline, quote+attribution, boilerplate, legal
// disclaimer, etc.), per the audit's content-decomposition finding.
// masterContent (kept) stays the flat, backward-compatible string every
// existing validation/derivative/QA check already reads; fieldsJson is the
// new structured source of truth the front end edits directly.
ensureColumn('projects', 'fieldsJson', 'TEXT');

// Round 60 — real authentication (Deliverable 14). accessCodeHash/Salt are
// null for every account created before this round (including the seeded
// demo account) until claimAccessCode() runs — see that function's own
// comment, right below, for how those get a code without ever exposing one
// silently server-side for a real (non-demo) account.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'accessCodeHash', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'accessCodeSalt', 'TEXT');

// Round 70 — Analytics Integration setup, per cxmedia-data-integration-
// architecture.md's single-connector model. `analyticsPathway` is one of
// 'snowflake' | 'sheets' | 'ga4' | '' (unset). The Snowflake fields below
// capture what a client's own data team would need to hand CX Experiences
// to provision a read-only Secure Data Share connector — this is a real,
// persisted setup REQUEST, not a live connector (none exists yet, per the
// doc's own "neither of these is built" language for the fallback tier,
// and Section 3's connector being unbuilt engineering work for Snowflake
// too). `analyticsStatus` tracks where that request stands: 'not_started' |
// 'requested' | 'connected' — 'connected' is set manually by CX Experiences
// once the real connector work happens, never by the client-facing form.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'analyticsPathway', "TEXT DEFAULT ''");
ensureColumn('accounts', 'analyticsStatus', "TEXT DEFAULT 'not_started'");
ensureColumn('accounts', 'analyticsSnowflakeAccountId', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'analyticsSnowflakeShareName', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'analyticsSnowflakeContact', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'analyticsFallbackNotes', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'analyticsRequestedAt', 'TEXT');
// Round 90 — GA4 gets its own two structured fields (Analytics Account ID,
// Property ID), same "dedicated fields, not just the generic fallback notes
// textarea" treatment Snowflake already gets above, added when a real
// client's GA4 account/property numbers needed to be recorded (Atlas Ocean
// Voyages' demo activation). Every other no-warehouse pathway (Meta,
// Google Ads, Adobe, Sheets) still only has the generic
// analyticsFallbackNotes field — GA4 is the one this round gave structure
// to, per the specific request, not a blanket upgrade to all four.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'analyticsGa4AccountId', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'analyticsGa4PropertyId', 'TEXT');

// Round 71 — Master UTM Configuration, within Analytics Integrations. Up to
// 8 selectable elements (from the 12-item VALID_UTM_ELEMENTS whitelist in
// the /utm-config route below), stored as an ordered JSON array — order
// matters, it's the query-string parameter order every campaign's generated
// tracking links use.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'utmMasterElementsJson', 'TEXT');

// Round 72 — auto-inserted per-channel tracking parameters, per direct
// instruction: a 1st-party Contact ID auto-inserted on email links (token
// text is editable since every ESP's merge-tag syntax differs — HubSpot,
// Klaviyo, Marketo, Mailchimp all spell this differently), and GCLID
// auto-inserted on Google Ads links (fixed syntax — Google's own ValueTrack
// `{gclid}` token, same thing Google Ads' own auto-tagging already relies
// on, so this defaults ON to match that platform default). See the
// /utm-config route below for how these combine with buildUtmLink()'s
// per-link checkboxes on the front end.
ensureColumn('accounts', 'utmAutoContactIdEnabled', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
ensureColumn('accounts', 'utmContactIdParam', "TEXT DEFAULT 'contact_id'");
ensureColumn('accounts', 'utmContactIdToken', "TEXT DEFAULT '{{contact_id}}'");
ensureColumn('accounts', 'utmAutoGclidEnabled', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 1');

// Round 55 — campaign_allocation_draws, closing the round-50 scoping doc's
// original design: a campaign can span multiple Media Plan allocations
// (multiple stages/channels) instead of the single flat allocationId/budget
// pair rounds 53-54 shipped as a "flat field now, normalize later" shortcut.
// campaigns.allocationId/fundingSource/budget stay on the table for backward
// compatibility with any already-seeded single-draw campaign data (read as a
// one-row legacy draw when no rows exist here yet) and because budget still
// doubles as the campaign's total spend figure everywhere else in this file
// (Analysis step, pacing math) — draws are the new source of truth for HOW
// that total breaks down across allocations, not a replacement for budget
// itself.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS campaign_allocation_draws (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    campaignId TEXT NOT NULL,
    allocationId TEXT NOT NULL,
    amount REAL,
    FOREIGN KEY (campaignId) REFERENCES campaigns(id),
    FOREIGN KEY (allocationId) REFERENCES media_plan_allocations(id)
  );
`);

// Round 102 — Channel Planning Extended Fields, Secure Client Workflow,
// Taxonomy, Marketing Calendar, per cxmedia-round102-channel-planning-
// extended-fields-marketing-calendar.md. Two new tables:
//
// account_taxonomies — the account-configurable dropdown value lists the
// doc's Section 3 calls for (Product Group, Creative Market, Audience, Buy
// Type, Media Type). AOV/Atlas Ocean Voyages already runs this exact field
// set operationally in its own Excel-based planning today — this is that
// existing vocabulary becoming real, editable, per-account dropdown data
// instead of a hardcoded travel-specific enum, so a non-travel account can
// define its own Product Group/Creative Market lists without a code change.
// One row per (accountId, taxonomyKey); label lets an account rename the
// generic slot to match its own vocabulary (e.g. "Product Line" instead of
// "Product Group") without touching the key the rest of the app reads by.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS account_taxonomies (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    taxonomyKey TEXT NOT NULL,
    label TEXT NOT NULL,
    valuesJson TEXT NOT NULL,
    updatedAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId)
  );
`);

// 2026-08-22 — quick-start templates for account_taxonomies above, per
// direct instruction: a brand-new account (no Channel Planning Detail
// history yet to auto-derive a taxonomy from — see cmpTaxonomyValues() in
// portal.html) had no way to set up real Product Group / Creative Focus
// Group values at all except uploading real historical media rows first, a
// real chicken-and-egg gap for "create proper campaigns that help us test
// copywriting" on a fresh account like Atlas. This registry is that
// starting point — never auto-applied, only offered; the account picks one
// (or starts blank) and can freely edit or fully replace every value before
// saving, same "brands can override everything" instruction.
// naicsHint is informational only (shown in the picker UI) — there is no
// per-account NAICS code stored anywhere in this build yet, so nothing
// auto-matches an account to a template today; that's real future work, not
// silently faked here. Starting with travel only, per direct instruction
// ("we should start with travel but then expand to include best guesses for
// each NAICS code") — new entries get added to this array as later rounds
// build out other industries; nothing invented beyond what was actually
// specified. creativeMarket is intentionally EMPTY on the Travel Agency
// template — only a Product Group list was given for that one; a Creative
// Focus Group list was specified for Cruise Line only, so that's the only
// one that ships with one, rather than reusing the cruise destinations list
// for an unrelated business without being asked to.
const TAXONOMY_TEMPLATES = [
  {
    key: 'cruise-line',
    label: 'Cruise Line',
    naicsHint: 'Travel — Cruise Lines (NAICS 483112, Deep Sea Passenger Transportation)',
    description: 'Ocean vs. River as the Product Group split, with the real destination markets a cruise line actually sells as Creative Focus Groups.',
    productGroup: ['Ocean', 'River'],
    creativeMarket: ['Caribbean', 'South America', 'Antarctica', 'Arctic', 'Mexico and Pacific Coast', 'South Pacific', 'Australia', 'Asia', 'Africa', 'Mediterranean', 'Greek Isles', 'Northern Europe', 'Baltic']
  },
  {
    key: 'travel-agency',
    label: 'Travel Agency',
    naicsHint: 'Travel — Online/Retail Travel Agency (NAICS 561510, Travel Agencies)',
    description: 'The core bookable categories a multi-product travel agent (e.g. an Expedia-style business) sells as Product Groups. No Creative Focus Group starting list yet for this one — define it directly for how this business actually segments its creative.',
    productGroup: ['Hotel', 'Air', 'Car', 'Cruise', 'Train', 'Local Activities'],
    creativeMarket: []
  }
];

// channel_planning_details — the doc's Section 1 extended per-channel
// planning entry: one row per vendor/insertion/drop, matching AOV's real
// MEDIA_PLANS_2H2026.xlsx row shape (Marketing Group/Partner/Budget/
// Drop-Hit-End Date/Audience/Product Year/Product Group/Creative Market/Buy
// Type/Media Type/Impressions on every channel tab, plus channel-specific
// sub-fields folded into detailsJson rather than a wide sparse column set —
// same "one JSON blob for the variable part" convention as
// trafficPackageJson/mediaMixJson above).
//
// allocationId is nullable and deliberately loose (not NOT NULL/no FK
// enforcement beyond the comment) — the doc's Section 1 budget-
// reconciliation note says each entry "becomes a draw against the
// campaign's channel allocation," but a real planning entry may not map
// cleanly 1:1 to an existing media_plan_allocations row (segmented Direct
// Mail channels, e.g., vs. this dataset's undifferentiated "Direct Mail"),
// so this stays null rather than force a guessed match — see
// seed-atlas-channel-plans.js for the same caution applied on import.
//
// status (draft|submitted|approved) is the doc's Section 2 per-entry
// approval gate — a client can save partial detail as Draft; it isn't
// treated as confirmed until a CX Experiences Media Ops user marks it
// Approved. enteredBy*/lastEditedBy* are the field-level provenance stamps
// Section 2 asks for instead of a separate client portal — both sides edit
// the same row, this just records who touched it last. The cost sub-fields
// living inside detailsJson (unitCost, postageCost, paperMfgCost,
// insertionCost) are the "sensitive vendor economics" Section 2 restricts to
// CX Experiences Media Ops only — enforced in the POST/PATCH routes below,
// not just left to the front end to hide.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS channel_planning_details (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    campaignId TEXT NOT NULL,
    allocationId TEXT,
    channel TEXT NOT NULL,
    partner TEXT,
    audience TEXT,
    buyType TEXT,
    mediaType TEXT,
    impressions REAL,
    dropDate TEXT,
    hitDate TEXT,
    endDate TEXT,
    productYear TEXT,
    productGroup TEXT,
    creativeMarket TEXT,
    budget REAL,
    detailsJson TEXT,
    status TEXT DEFAULT 'draft',
    enteredByRole TEXT,
    enteredByName TEXT,
    lastEditedByRole TEXT,
    lastEditedByName TEXT,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    updatedAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (campaignId) REFERENCES campaigns(id),
    FOREIGN KEY (allocationId) REFERENCES media_plan_allocations(id)
  );
`);

// Round 64 — Creative Jobs (grouping & prioritizing creative requests).
// Per direct instruction: a Campaign ID already exists (campaigns.id,
// CMP-...) — what's new is a Creative Job ID for an actual request sent to
// the creative team (from print-specs.html, ooh-specs.html, or eventually
// portal.html's own Project flow), so requests can be grouped by the
// campaign they belong to and sorted by priority, matching a real creative
// team's intake queue rather than a loose pile of emails/Slack messages.
// specsJson is a full snapshot of the selected spec at request time (not
// just a reference to the catalog) — the catalog can change later
// (a publication's trim size gets revised, a format gets retired) without
// silently rewriting what a creative team was actually asked to build
// against.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS creative_jobs (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    campaignId TEXT NOT NULL,
    channel TEXT NOT NULL,
    deliveryPlatform TEXT,
    sizeOrLength TEXT,
    aspect TEXT,
    specSummary TEXT,
    specsJson TEXT,
    priority TEXT DEFAULT 'Medium',
    status TEXT DEFAULT 'Pending',
    requestedBy TEXT,
    notes TEXT,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    updatedAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId),
    FOREIGN KEY (campaignId) REFERENCES campaigns(id)
  );
`);
const CREATIVE_JOB_PRIORITY_ORDER = { Urgent: 0, High: 1, Medium: 2, Low: 3 };

// Added 2026-07-27 (round 68) — the next step in the creative process: a
// per-job copy workspace (see portal.html's copy-workspace panel).
// workingCopy is whatever the team actually edited it to (starts as the
// round-65 recommendation, but is meant to be refined); copyApproved/
// copyApprovedAt gate the job from moving past 'Requested' — see the
// PATCH /api/creative-jobs/:id handler below, direct instruction: "copy
// must be approved before going to creative next step."
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'workingCopy', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'copyApproved', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'copyApprovedAt', 'TEXT');

// Round 73 — Ad Trafficking, per direct instruction: "Creative Jobs are the
// next step after copy is approved as a unique step. Links and QR codes
// will be Ad Trafficking that follow creative. Links and Creative are sent
// to the trafficking team when all assets are approved." assetApproved is
// deliberately a SEPARATE flag from copyApproved above (confirmed via
// direct follow-up) — copy approval (the text) and final creative-asset
// approval (the finished design/artwork) are different checkpoints; see the
// PATCH /api/creative-jobs/:id gate below requiring copyApproved before
// assetApproved can be set. trackingUrl is this job's own generated UTM
// link (round 71/72's builder, now persisted per-job here) rather than a
// one-off preview only — Send to Trafficking (below) bundles exactly this
// column across every job on a campaign.
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'assetApproved', 'INTEGER DEFAULT 0');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'assetApprovedAt', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'trackingUrl', 'TEXT');

// Round 74 — Creative Jobs restructured into a designer-facing task grid,
// per direct instruction. Two changes: (1) dueDate/deliveryDate ("date
// assets sent to publisher") are new, manually-set date fields — nothing
// upstream computes these; (2) status moves to a fixed 4-value vocabulary
// (Pending/In Progress/Pending Approval/Complete) instead of free text —
// the migration below renames every existing 'Requested' row (this table's
// old default) to 'Pending' so pre-round-74 data reads correctly under the
// new vocabulary. Safe to run on every boot — a no-op once migrated.
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'dueDate', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'deliveryDate', 'TEXT');
db.exec(`UPDATE creative_jobs SET status = 'Pending' WHERE status = 'Requested'`);

// Round 76 — per cxmedia-campaign-analytics-strategy.md Section 3a: the
// "magazines vs. magazines" / "direct mail vs. direct mail" comparison this
// doc scoped needs to know which SPECIFIC placement within a multi-placement
// campaign performed, not just one flat number for the whole campaign. Same
// three fields as campaigns.actualSpend/actualImpressions/actualConversions,
// same manual "no live feed" entry pattern — additive, so a campaign with
// only one creative job (the common case) can keep using its campaign-level
// actuals untouched; these are optional per-job detail on top.
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'actualSpend', 'REAL');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'actualImpressions', 'INTEGER');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'actualConversions', 'INTEGER');

// Round 132c17 (2026-08-17), per direct instruction: "All creative assets
// produced require a Job Id with the due date... Jobs may be distributed to
// different skills based on type of creative (e.g., Print, Digital Ads,
// Website and Email, Video, Radio, Out-of-home). All creative must come back
// for final CX Relevance Scoring." jobNumber/jobCode are set once at
// creation (see the creative-jobs POST handler); skill is derived from the
// job's channel via mapChannelToSkill(). cxRelevanceScore/ScoredAt/Notes
// are the final gate — a job reaching status 'Complete' isn't done until
// these are set (see the new cx-relevance-score endpoint below).
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'jobNumber', 'INTEGER');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'jobCode', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'skill', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'cxRelevanceScore', 'REAL');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'cxRelevanceScoredAt', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('creative_jobs', 'cxRelevanceNotes', 'TEXT');

// 2026-08-22 — Copy decision log ("structured approve/reject-with-reason
// capture", first item off the CX & Copywriting product-vision roadmap).
// creative_jobs.copyApproved above only ever stores the CURRENT state —
// approving copy after a prior rejection overwrites the rejection with no
// trace of it, and there was never anywhere to capture WHY something was
// rejected in the first place. That "why" is exactly the training signal
// the vision doc describes ("rejecting isn't a dead end... that reason is
// what makes tomorrow's first draft need less correction than today's") —
// today it was being thrown away. This table is a permanent, append-only
// log of every copy decision on a job (one row per decision, oldest to
// newest), independent of creative_jobs.copyApproved which still tracks
// only the current/latest state for the existing status-gate logic below.
// decidedBy is the session's accountId/memberId pair rendered as a single
// string (best-effort attribution — not a foreign key, since a decision
// should stay on the record even if the deciding member is later removed).
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS creative_job_decisions (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    jobId TEXT NOT NULL,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    approved INTEGER NOT NULL,
    reason TEXT,
    decidedBy TEXT,
    decidedAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (jobId) REFERENCES creative_jobs(id)
  );
`);

// 2026-08-22 — Multi-candidate "interview" panel (CX & Copywriting vision
// roadmap item 4, the last and biggest item on the list — "run the same
// brief across multiple models and subagents, side by side... we want the
// best of the best by function"). Honest scope note, since this is the
// item most likely to get overclaimed: this deployment has exactly one
// AI vendor credentialed (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, see generateMessagingCopyViaAI
// above) — there is no OpenAI/Gemini key anywhere in this codebase, so a
// real cross-vendor "GPT vs Gemini vs Claude" bake-off isn't something this
// build can honestly claim yet. What IS real and shippable today: running
// the SAME brief through several distinct copywriting "subagents" — each a
// separate generation call with its own strategic angle (direct-response /
// brand-story / proof-and-trust), which is exactly the "subagents" half of
// Todd's ask — then scoring every candidate with the same scoreDraftCopy()
// used for the pre-decision score (item 2), so the panel ranks them on the
// same real relevance/compliance axes a human reviewer would use, not a
// fabricated "AI vote." The panel ALSO lists the not-yet-configured vendors
// (OpenAI, Google) honestly as "not configured on this deployment" rather
// than hiding them — so the UI is already the shape a real multi-vendor
// panel will have the day those credentials exist, with zero redesign
// needed then. Append-only log, same convention as creative_job_decisions:
// candidatesJson is the full panel result at the moment it ran;
// selectedCandidateKey/selectedBy/selectedAt are filled in later, once, by
// the separate /select endpoint below (an interview can be run without ever
// being acted on — that's fine, it's just not reflected in workingCopy).
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS creative_job_interviews (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    jobId TEXT NOT NULL,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    requestedBy TEXT,
    candidatesJson TEXT NOT NULL,
    selectedCandidateKey TEXT,
    selectedBy TEXT,
    selectedAt TEXT,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (jobId) REFERENCES creative_jobs(id)
  );
`);
// 2026-08-22, later same day — per direct instruction: "we will need to
// incorporate the test product type as this will expand to other features
// as we build." The blind-test/interview mechanism was built generically
// (runCandidateInterview() takes any campaign+account and any candidate
// set — nothing about it is copy-specific except the angle briefs), and
// Todd's already flagging that copy is only the FIRST feature to run
// through it — relevance scoring, subject lines, or anything else judged
// by multiple independent passes will want the same blind-test mechanism
// later. productType tags which feature produced a given interview row so
// the admin view (and any future per-feature reporting) can group/filter
// by it from day one, instead of every row silently being assumed to mean
// "copywriting" forever. ensureColumn (not baked into the CREATE TABLE
// above) so this lands correctly on a deployment that already created the
// table under the prior shape. INTERVIEW_PRODUCT_TYPES is the registry new
// product types get added to as they're built — see the copywriting
// interview endpoint below for the one real entry today.
ensureColumn('creative_job_interviews', 'productType', "TEXT DEFAULT 'copywriting'");
// 2026-08-22, later same day — per direct instruction: "we may run into
// scenarios when no option is selected. This is a good time to insert the
// feedback loop. What don't they like and what do they want to change or is
// one option close enough to edit manually." Same append-only-row
// convention as everything else on this table: feedback is recorded ONCE
// per interview (a second submission overwrites it — an interview only
// gets re-run, not re-critiqued twice), via the new POST .../feedback
// endpoint below. feedbackType distinguishes the two real scenarios Todd
// named ('none_selected' — nothing worked, free-text only; 'edit_manually'
// — one candidate was close, applied to workingCopy as a starting point,
// same as /select, with the gap explained in feedbackNote) so a future
// pass can tell "needs a different angle entirely" apart from "was close."
ensureColumn('creative_job_interviews', 'feedbackNote', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('creative_job_interviews', 'feedbackType', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('creative_job_interviews', 'feedbackBy', 'TEXT');
ensureColumn('creative_job_interviews', 'feedbackAt', 'TEXT');
const INTERVIEW_PRODUCT_TYPES = {
  copywriting: { label: 'Copywriting', description: 'Blind draft copy candidates, scored on relevance + compliance.' }
  // Future product types register here as they're built — e.g. a subject-line
  // tester or a landing-page-variant tester would each add one entry, reusing
  // runCandidateInterview()'s same generate → score → rank → log shape.
};

// 2026-08-22 — the multi-model Brand Voice contest. Deliberately a SEPARATE
// table from creative_job_interviews rather than a new INTERVIEW_PRODUCT_TYPES
// entry: every product type that table was designed for (see its own comment)
// is scoped to a specific creative job/campaign (jobId NOT NULL, FK'd to
// creative_jobs) — a subject-line tester or landing-page tester both still
// belong to one job. A Brand Voice contest belongs to the ACCOUNT, not any
// one job — there's no campaign or creative job to attach it to, and forcing
// a NULL/placeholder jobId onto an existing NOT-NULL FK'd column would be
// exactly the kind of misleading schema shortcut this build has avoided
// everywhere else. Same append-only-row convention, same feedback-loop shape
// (feedbackNote/Type/By/At) as creative_job_interviews, just without the
// job-scoped fields that don't apply here.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS account_voice_interviews (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    requestedBy TEXT,
    candidatesJson TEXT NOT NULL,
    selectedCandidateKey TEXT,
    selectedBy TEXT,
    selectedAt TEXT,
    feedbackNote TEXT,
    feedbackType TEXT,
    feedbackBy TEXT,
    feedbackAt TEXT,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL
  );
`);
// Three genuinely distinct brand-voice directions — not three re-rolls of one
// prompt, same "real subagents" discipline as INTERVIEW_SUBAGENT_ANGLES
// above. Each is a full personality a brand could plausibly commit to, not a
// minor wording variation of the others.
const BRAND_VOICE_SUBAGENT_ANGLES = [
  { key: 'confident-authority', label: 'Confident Authority', vendor: 'Anthropic', model: 'claude-sonnet-4-5',
    brief: 'A voice that leads with the plain claim, then the reasoning — never hedges with "we strive to" or "we believe." Speaks like the expert in the room. Short, declarative sentences. Confidence comes from specificity, not volume.' },
  { key: 'warm-storyteller', label: 'Warm Storyteller', vendor: 'Anthropic', model: 'claude-sonnet-4-5',
    brief: 'A voice that opens with a specific, sensory moment — never a generic greeting — and lets the point arrive as the natural next beat of the story, not a hard pivot to a sales line. Warmth comes from specificity (a real detail), never from generic friendliness.' },
  { key: 'bold-modern', label: 'Bold Modern', vendor: 'Anthropic', model: 'claude-sonnet-4-5',
    brief: 'A voice that is energetic and contemporary without being juvenile — punchy sentence rhythm, confident fragments where they earn their place, zero corporate hedging. Reads like a brand that knows exactly who it is and isn\'t trying to please everyone.' }
];
// Same "architected for, not yet credentialed" honesty as
// INTERVIEW_UNCONFIGURED_VENDORS above — reused verbatim rather than
// duplicated, since it's the exact same vendor pool for the exact same
// reason (only ANTHROPIC_API_KEY exists on this deployment today).
//
// "Critical customer-facing messages" — per direct instruction, derived from
// this account's EXISTING brand inputs rather than a new required field:
// Products & Services (what to actually talk about) and the approved Brand &
// Product Keyword chips (the emotional/voice signal already validated for
// this account) are the two account-level fields already asking for exactly
// this. Tone Anchors / Words-to-avoid / anti-example are tracked client-side
// today (see generateVoiceDraft() in portal.html) rather than persisted per-
// account — this endpoint accepts them as optional request-body context so
// the contest still benefits from them when the client has them picked,
// without inventing a new server-side field this round just to hold them.
function brandVoiceCriticalMessagesContext(account, extra){
  const lines = [];
  if (account.productsServices) lines.push(`Products & Services: ${String(account.productsServices).slice(0, 800)}`);
  let brandKeywords = null, productKeywords = null;
  try {
    const parsed = account.brandKeywordsJson ? JSON.parse(account.brandKeywordsJson) : null;
    if (parsed){ brandKeywords = parsed.brand || null; productKeywords = parsed.product || null; }
