  // category-specific word like "Consulting" or "Execution". A real budget
  // category essentially never starts with the literal word "total," so a
  // prefix match here is safe -- and deliberately narrower than a plain
  // substring match, since a real category can legitimately CONTAIN the
  // word "total" without being one (e.g. this same file's "Consumer Print
  // Advertising - 13% of total ", a real rollup that's correctly identified
  // by the numeric signals instead, not this one).
  if (/^(grand\s+)?(sub)?total\b/i.test(t)) return true;
  const genericWords = ['total', 'totals', 'grand', 'sub', 'subtotal', 'subtotals', 'marketing', 'budget', 'spend', 'media', 'annual', 'overall', 'all', 'combined', 'summary'];
  const words = t.toLowerCase().split(/\s+/);
  return words.length <= 3 && words.includes('total') && words.every(w => genericWords.includes(w));
}
const MARKETING_BUDGET_STATUS_TOKEN_RE = /^(working|non-working|non-woring|working[\s-]*(partner|audience|channel|trade))$/i;
const MARKETING_BUDGET_TARGET_FIELDS = [
  { key: 'category', label: 'Category / Line Item' },
  { key: 'status', label: 'Working / Non-Working' },
  ...MARKETING_BUDGET_MONTHS.map(m => ({ key: m, label: MARKETING_BUDGET_MONTH_LABELS[m] })),
  { key: 'total', label: 'Annual Total' }
];

// Verilume's own standard budget taxonomy, per direct instruction: "we're
// building for multiple industries and terms" — a client's raw category
// text ("Conde Nast Traveller", "SEM-REDUCED", "Ralio / social for trade")
// is real, useful data, but it's THIS client's own vocabulary, not a set
// any cross-account rollup, benchmark, or MMM handoff could use directly.
// This is a first version of that standard set, not represented as an
// industry-official taxonomy — it's Verilume's own, open to revision. Kept
// deliberately separate from MMM_CATEGORIES (travel-specific, built for a
// different consumer) and from CHANNELS (portal.html's 12-item campaign-
// channel list) — this needs to also hold real non-media budget lines
// (consulting, trade, production, fulfillment, data ops) that a real
// annual budget contains and neither of those lists were built to carry.
// Round 132bx follow-on (2026-08-15), per repeated direct correction —
// "Video is 5 distinct channels: Linear, CTV, OTV, OTV, Social" and "Video
// is still video/ctv without other options" — 'Video/CTV' used to be ONE
// merged budget category covering both CTV and OTV streaming video. Two
// earlier rounds tried to fix this by broadening VERILUME_TO_MMM_CHANNEL_MAP
// (the crosswalk into Campaign Creation's channel picker) instead of this
// list — that was the wrong list to touch: this constant is what actually
// populates the "Map to a Verilume category" and "+ Add category" pickers
// on the Marketing Budget Upload screen itself, so neither earlier fix ever
// showed up there. Split 'Video/CTV' into its own 'CTV' and 'OTV' entries
// here — 'Linear TV' and 'Social' were already separate entries in this
// same list, so this completes the 4-way split into Linear TV / CTV / OTV /
// Social exactly as directly requested, all independently selectable and
// mappable on this screen.
const VERILUME_BUDGET_CATEGORIES = [
  'Search', 'Social', 'Display/Programmatic', 'CTV', 'OTV', 'Email', 'PR/Earned',
  'Linear TV', 'Direct Mail', 'Events', 'Podcasts', 'Radio', 'Magazines/Print',
  'Out-of-Home', 'Partner/Affiliate Media',
  'Consulting & Professional Fees', 'Trade/B2B Marketing',
  'Production (Photo/Video/Creative)', 'Fulfillment & Collateral',
  'Data, Technology & Analytics', 'Other/Uncategorized'
];

// Round 132bx follow-on (2026-08-15), per direct instruction: a confirmed
// Marketing Budget Upload's identified (nonzero) categories should drive
// which Campaign Creation channels are active. Campaign Creation's channel
// picker speaks a different, travel-specific taxonomy (MMM_CATEGORIES, 22
// entries, defined in portal.html and mirrored nowhere in this file today) —
// this is the crosswalk from Verilume's 20 budget categories to that
// taxonomy, my own reasonable mapping, not something specified, so flagged
// here rather than decided silently:
//  - Several Verilume categories have no real MMM_CATEGORIES equivalent
//    because they're not a paid-media channel at all (Consulting &
//    Professional Fees, Production, Fulfillment & Collateral, Data/
//    Technology & Analytics, Other/Uncategorized) — they map to nothing and
//    never activate or deactivate a channel.
//  - 'Events' has no clean single match either; mapped to 'Field / ABM'
//    (MMM_CATEGORIES' own comment describes it as covering "trade show
//    presence") as the closest fit — worth a real confirm, not confident.
//  - A Verilume category maps to MORE than one MMM channel where Verilume's
//    taxonomy is coarser (e.g. one 'Search' category covers both 'Brand
//    Search' and 'Non-Brand Search'; one 'Direct Mail' covers all three MMM
//    Direct Mail variants) — every channel on that list activates together;
//    there's no way from this data alone to tell Brand Search spend apart
//    from Non-Brand Search spend within a single 'Search' budget line.
//  - Round 132bx follow-on (2026-08-15), per direct correction: "Video is a
//    broad category" — 'Video/CTV' originally only activated 'OTV' and
//    'CTV'. A single client budget line called "Video" often also funds
//    social-platform video (in-feed/Stories/Reels video buys, which MMM
//    tracks under 'Paid Social', not a separate video channel) — same
//    coarser-Verilume-category pattern as Search/Direct Mail above, so
//    'Video/CTV' now activates 'Paid Social' too. Harmless overlap with the
//    'Social' row below when both are present (union of an already-active
//    channel is a no-op); the real effect is for an account that reports
//    all video under one "Video" line with no separate Social line at all.
//  - Round 132bx follow-on #2 (2026-08-15), per direct correction: "Video is
//    5 distinct channels: Linear, CTV, OTV, Social" — the 'Video/CTV' →
//    ['OTV', 'CTV', 'Paid Social', 'Linear TV'] union tried here originally
//    was the wrong fix: it papered over 'Video/CTV' still being ONE merged
//    Verilume budget category by activating every video-adjacent MMM
//    channel whenever it had any spend, rather than actually letting a
//    client report CTV and OTV as the distinct line items they are.
//  - Round 132bx follow-on #3 (2026-08-15) — the real fix: split
//    VERILUME_BUDGET_CATEGORIES' 'Video/CTV' into its own 'CTV' and 'OTV'
//    entries (see that constant's own comment). Each now maps 1:1 to its
//    matching MMM channel below, same as every other clean Verilume↔MMM
//    pair — no more union-of-everything-video-related guessing needed once
//    the categories themselves are granular enough to tell apart. The old
//    'Video/CTV' key is kept working here (not removed) purely so any
//    pre-existing historical line item still tagged with the now-retired
//    merged name keeps activating what it always did — 'Video/CTV' itself
//    is no longer offered as a choice anywhere in the UI, so no NEW data
//    can be tagged with it going forward.
const VERILUME_TO_MMM_CHANNEL_MAP = {
  'Search': ['Brand Search', 'Non-Brand Search'],
  'Social': ['Paid Social'],
  'Display/Programmatic': ['Programmatic Display'],
  'CTV': ['CTV'],
  'OTV': ['OTV'],
  'Video/CTV': ['OTV', 'CTV', 'Paid Social', 'Linear TV'], // legacy — retired category, kept for historical data only
  'Email': ['Internal Email', 'Email — Remarketing'],
  'PR/Earned': ['PR'],
  'Linear TV': ['Linear TV'],
  'Direct Mail': ['Direct Mail — Prospects', 'Direct Mail — Past Guests', 'Direct Mail — Inquiries'],
  'Events': ['Field / ABM'],
  'Podcasts': ['Podcasts'],
  'Radio': ['Radio'],
  'Magazines/Print': ['Magazines', 'Newspapers'],
  'Out-of-Home': ['Out-of-Home'],
  'Partner/Affiliate Media': ['Partner Media', 'Retail Media'],
  'Trade/B2B Marketing': ['Field / ABM'],
  // No MMM channel: 'Consulting & Professional Fees', 'Production (Photo/
  // Video/Creative)', 'Fulfillment & Collateral', 'Data, Technology &
  // Analytics', 'Other/Uncategorized'.
};
// These three (see RECOMMENDED_CHANNELS_DEMAND_FULFILLMENT and
// OWNED_MEDIA_CHANNELS in portal.html) are documented elsewhere in this
// codebase as "always-on, budget not tied to one campaign" — Brand Search
// captures demand other media already created, Internal Email is owned
// media, and plain Remarketing is pixel-based retargeting of a campaign's
// own site visitors. Gating any of these off just because one Marketing
// Budget Upload happened to show $0 in the (imperfectly-mapped) category
// that feeds it contradicts that existing design, so they stay active
// regardless of what a given upload identifies. Flagged as a judgment call.
const MMM_CHANNELS_ALWAYS_ACTIVE = ['Brand Search', 'Internal Email', 'Remarketing'];

// Recomputes and replaces this account's active-channel set from a just-
// confirmed upload's byVerilumeCategory rollup (nonzero/overridden totals
// only — a category present at $0 does NOT activate its mapped channels).
// Wholesale replace, not a merge: this reflects "as of the latest confirmed
// upload," so a channel that lost its budget on a re-upload correctly drops
// out rather than sticking around from an earlier confirm.
function recomputeAccountActiveChannels(accountId, byVerilumeCategory){
  const active = new Set(MMM_CHANNELS_ALWAYS_ACTIVE);
  Object.entries(byVerilumeCategory || {}).forEach(([verilumeCategory, total]) => {
    if (!(total > 0)) return;
    (VERILUME_TO_MMM_CHANNEL_MAP[verilumeCategory] || []).forEach(ch => active.add(ch));
  });
  const now = new Date().toISOString();
  // node:sqlite's DatabaseSync has no .transaction() helper (unlike
  // better-sqlite3) — every call here is already synchronous on its own, so
  // a wrapping transaction isn't needed for correctness, just kept as plain
  // sequential statements to match how the rest of this file uses db.
  db.prepare('DELETE FROM account_active_channels WHERE accountId = ?').run(accountId);
  const ins = db.prepare('INSERT INTO account_active_channels (accountId, channel, updatedAt) VALUES (?, ?, ?)');
  active.forEach(ch => ins.run(accountId, ch, now));
  return [...active];
}

// Shared by the confirm-categories endpoint (to (re)compute this account's
// active channels the moment an upload becomes confirmed) and the final-
// results GET endpoint (to display the same numbers) — kept in one place so
// they can't drift apart.
function computeByVerilumeCategoryForUpload(uploadId){
  const lineItems = db.prepare('SELECT category, annualTotal, verilumeCategory FROM marketing_budget_line_items WHERE uploadId = ?').all(uploadId);
  const byCategory = {};
  lineItems.forEach(li => {
    if (!byCategory[li.category]) byCategory[li.category] = { verilumeCategory: li.verilumeCategory || null, annualTotal: li.annualTotal ?? null, sumOfMonths: 0 };
    if (li.annualTotal == null) byCategory[li.category].sumOfMonths += 0; // months are summed by the caller's own query when needed; annualTotal is the common case
  });
  // Re-derive sumOfMonths properly (annualTotal isn't always present per row).
  const monthRows = db.prepare('SELECT category, amount FROM marketing_budget_line_items WHERE uploadId = ? AND month IS NOT NULL').all(uploadId);
  monthRows.forEach(r => { if (byCategory[r.category]) byCategory[r.category].sumOfMonths += (r.amount || 0); });
  const byVerilumeCategory = {};
  Object.values(byCategory).forEach(c => {
    const key = c.verilumeCategory || 'Other/Uncategorized';
    if (!byVerilumeCategory[key]) byVerilumeCategory[key] = 0;
    byVerilumeCategory[key] += (c.annualTotal != null ? c.annualTotal : c.sumOfMonths);
  });
  const overrideRows = db.prepare('SELECT verilumeCategory, overrideTotal FROM marketing_budget_category_overrides WHERE uploadId = ?').all(uploadId);
  overrideRows.forEach(r => { byVerilumeCategory[r.verilumeCategory] = r.overrideTotal; });
  return byVerilumeCategory;
}

// Heuristic ONLY — every result here is a 'suggested' starting point a
// human confirms or corrects, never applied automatically. Matches on
// normalized raw category text; first matching rule wins; 'Other/
// Uncategorized' is the honest fallback rather than forcing a specific
// wrong guess.
function mbuSuggestVerilumeCategory(rawCategory){
  const s = String(rawCategory || '').toLowerCase();
  const rules = [
    [/\bsem\b|\bsearch\b|\bppc\b|\badwords\b|\bgoogle ads\b/, 'Search'],
    [/\bsocial\b/, 'Social'],
    [/\bdisplay\b|\bprogrammatic\b|\bbanner\b/, 'Display/Programmatic'],
    // Round 132bx follow-on #3 (2026-08-15) — split from one merged
    // 'Video/CTV' rule now that the category itself is split (see
    // VERILUME_BUDGET_CATEGORIES). CTV gets its own specific keyword match;
    // OTV also catches the generic 'video' term as a forgiving fallback —
    // round #3 originally left bare "video" unmatched on purpose (an honest
    // Exception rather than a guess), but that turned out to be too eager
    // to block: a common real-world label like "Video Advertising" or
    // "Digital Video" now silently became a NEW required Exception it
    // never used to be, which can leave "Submit as final" unable to
    // complete without an obvious reason why (round #4 direct bug report).
    // OTV is the more general/common bucket for undifferentiated "video"
    // spend, so it's the better forgiving default; still fully editable,
    // and specific CTV language still wins since that rule is checked
    // first.
    [/\bctv\b|\bconnected tv\b/, 'CTV'],
    [/\bott\b|\botv\b|\bstreaming\b|\bvideo\b/, 'OTV'],
    [/\bemail\b|\be-?mail\b/, 'Email'],
    [/\bpr\b|\bpublic relations\b|\binquir(y|ies)\b/, 'PR/Earned'],
    [/\blinear tv\b|\bbroadcast\b/, 'Linear TV'],
    [/\bdirect mail\b|\bdm\b|\bmailer\b|\bpast guest\b|\binsert/, 'Direct Mail'],
    [/\bevent(s)?\b|\btrade show\b/, 'Events'],
    [/\bpodcast/, 'Podcasts'],
    [/\bradio\b/, 'Radio'],
    [/\bmagazine\b|\bnewspaper\b|\bprint\b|\btraveller\b|\btravel\s*&?\s*leisure\b|\bfood\s*&?\s*wine\b|\bgarden\s*&?\s*gun\b|\bjournal\b|\bafar\b/, 'Magazines/Print'],
    [/\booh\b|\bout.of.home\b|\bbillboard\b/, 'Out-of-Home'],
    [/\bpartner\b|\baffiliate\b|\btravel leaders\b|\bcruise strategies\b/, 'Partner/Affiliate Media'],
    [/\bconsult/, 'Consulting & Professional Fees'],
    [/\btrade\b/, 'Trade/B2B Marketing'],
    [/\bphoto\b|\bvideo shoot\b|\bshoot\b|\bproduction\b|\bcreative\b/, 'Production (Photo/Video/Creative)'],
    [/\bcollateral\b|\bfulfillment\b|\bflyer\b|\bbrochure\b|\bfield\b|\bcalendar\b/, 'Fulfillment & Collateral'],
    [/\bdatabase\b|\bepsilon\b|\bmodeling\b|\bmatchback\b|\b4cite\b|\bdata\b|\btechnology\b|\bnavistone\b/, 'Data, Technology & Analytics']
  ];
  const hit = rules.find(([re]) => re.test(s));
  // matched:false means no real rule fired — this is the honest "we
  // couldn't identify this one" fallback, not a real suggestion. The
  // client-facing single-screen review (Round 132bx cont'd) uses this flag
  // to decide what counts as an "exception" needing a human decision,
  // rather than treating every fallback-to-Other/Uncategorized guess as
  // confidently mapped.
  return hit ? { category: hit[1], confidence: 'suggested', matched: true, source: 'rule' } : { category: 'Other/Uncategorized', confidence: 'suggested', matched: false, source: 'rule' };
}

// Round 132bx follow-on (2026-08-15), per direct instruction: "we should
// remember previous upload mapping decisions by account and automatically
// map the next time recognized." Every time an account confirms a raw-
// category → Verilume-category mapping (confirm-categories below), that
// decision is remembered here per account, keyed on a normalized version of
// the raw category text — so the SAME line-item name in next year's (or
// next month's) file auto-suggests the choice the client already made,
// instead of asking again or falling back to the generic rule-based
// heuristic above. A learned mapping still only ever produces a
// 'suggested' result the client can override on the review screen (same as
// the rule-based heuristic) — nothing here writes a category mapping
// without a human having chosen it at least once, and re-confirming a
// different mapping for the same raw text simply overwrites the memory
// going forward.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS account_category_mapping_memory (
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    rawCategoryNormalized TEXT NOT NULL,
    rawCategoryDisplay TEXT NOT NULL,
    verilumeCategory TEXT NOT NULL,
    updatedAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (accountId, rawCategoryNormalized),
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId)
  );
`);
// Normalizes a raw category label for matching across uploads: lowercase,
// trim, collapse internal whitespace. Deliberately simple/exact-ish rather
// than fuzzy — a real behavior change (client renaming "Search - Brand" to
// "Search Brand") should surface as a fresh exception to confirm once, not
// silently inherit an old mapping that may no longer apply.
function mbuNormalizeCategoryText(raw){
  return String(raw || '').trim().toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g, ' ');
}
function mbuRememberCategoryMapping(accountId, rawCategory, verilumeCategory){
  const normalized = mbuNormalizeCategoryText(rawCategory);
  if (!normalized || !verilumeCategory) return;
  const now = new Date().toISOString();
  db.prepare(`INSERT INTO account_category_mapping_memory (accountId, rawCategoryNormalized, rawCategoryDisplay, verilumeCategory, updatedAt) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
    ON CONFLICT(accountId, rawCategoryNormalized) DO UPDATE SET rawCategoryDisplay = excluded.rawCategoryDisplay, verilumeCategory = excluded.verilumeCategory, updatedAt = excluded.updatedAt`)
    .run(accountId, normalized, rawCategory, verilumeCategory, now);
}
// Returns { category, confidence: 'learned', matched: true, source: 'learned', learnedAt } or null.
// Checked BEFORE the rule-based heuristic in the category-mapping GET below
// — a client's own prior decision for this exact raw text is a stronger
// signal than a generic keyword rule, so it wins when both would apply.
function mbuRecallCategoryMapping(accountId, rawCategory){
  const normalized = mbuNormalizeCategoryText(rawCategory);
  if (!normalized) return null;
  const row = db.prepare('SELECT verilumeCategory, updatedAt FROM account_category_mapping_memory WHERE accountId = ? AND rawCategoryNormalized = ?').get(accountId, normalized);
  if (!row) return null;
  return { category: row.verilumeCategory, confidence: 'learned', matched: true, source: 'learned', learnedAt: row.updatedAt };
}

function mbuNumberOrNull(raw){
  if (raw == null) return null;
  const cleaned = String(raw).replace(/[$,\s%]/g, '');
  if (cleaned === '') return null;
  const n = Number(cleaned);
  return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : null;
}

// Scans one row (array of cell strings) and returns how many cells match a
// month name/abbreviation — the one signal a client's own header text is
// very likely to contain no matter how the rest of the file is laid out.
function mbuMonthMatchCount(row){
  let n = 0;
  (row || []).forEach(cell => {
    const s = String(cell || '').trim();
    if (!s) return;
    if (Object.values(MARKETING_BUDGET_MONTH_RE).some(re => re.test(s))) n++;
  });
  return n;
}

// For one header-like row, map each column index to a recognized target
// field (month or total) via regex on that exact cell's text. Category and
// Status are deliberately NOT auto-mapped here — no header text in the real
// file this was built against reliably names those columns ("Marketing",
// "Budget 2026" appear where "Category"/"Status" would be expected), so
// claiming a confident auto-map there would be a guess dressed up as a
// finding. They're offered as heuristic 'suggested' matches instead, scored
// separately by mbuSuggestCategoryAndStatusColumns.
function mbuMapHeaderRow(row){
  const map = {};
  const totalCandidates = [];
  (row || []).forEach((cell, idx) => {
    const s = String(cell || '').trim();
    if (!s) return;
    const monthKey = Object.keys(MARKETING_BUDGET_MONTH_RE).find(k => MARKETING_BUDGET_MONTH_RE[k].test(s));
    if (monthKey){ map[idx] = { key: monthKey, confidence: 'auto', matchedOn: s }; return; }
    if (MARKETING_BUDGET_TOTAL_RE.test(s)){ totalCandidates.push({ idx, matchedOn: s }); return; }
  });
  // Some real files repeat a total-like label twice (e.g. an early
  // "Budget 2026" summary column AND a trailing "TOTAL" column at the end
  // of the month run). Only the LAST one is treated as the authoritative
  // per-row total — it sits immediately after the month columns, where an
  // annual total column actually belongs; an earlier match is far more
  // often a block-level label that isn't populated on every detail row.
  if (totalCandidates.length){
    const last = totalCandidates[totalCandidates.length - 1];
    map[last.idx] = { key: 'total', confidence: 'auto', matchedOn: last.matchedOn };
  }
  return map;
}

// Heuristic-only: does this column's VALUES (not its header) look like a
// small, repeating set of classification tokens (WORKING / NON-WORKING /
// WORKING PARTNER / etc.)? And is there a column whose values are mostly
// distinct short text strings, i.e. plausibly line-item names? Both are
// reported as 'suggested' confidence, never 'auto' — a human confirms
// before either is used.
function mbuSuggestCategoryAndStatusColumns(dataRows, colCount){
  let statusCol = null, statusHits = 0;
  let categoryCol = null, categoryDistinctCount = -1;
  for (let c = 0; c < colCount; c++){
    const values = dataRows.map(r => String(r[c] || '').trim()).filter(Boolean);
    if (!values.length) continue;
    const statusMatches = values.filter(v => MARKETING_BUDGET_STATUS_TOKEN_RE.test(v)).length;
    if (statusMatches > statusHits && statusMatches >= Math.max(2, Math.floor(values.length * 0.3))){
      statusHits = statusMatches; statusCol = c;
    }
    const distinct = new Set(values.filter(v => !MARKETING_BUDGET_STATUS_TOKEN_RE.test(v) && !/^\d/.test(v))).size;
    const numericLike = values.filter(v => /^[\d.,$%\s-]+$/.test(v)).length;
    if (numericLike < values.length * 0.3 && distinct > categoryDistinctCount && c !== statusCol){
      categoryDistinctCount = distinct; categoryCol = c;
    }
  }
  return {
    status: statusCol != null ? { colIdx: statusCol, confidence: 'suggested', reason: `${statusHits} of ${dataRows.length} rows contain a Working/Non-Working style token` } : null,
    category: categoryCol != null ? { colIdx: categoryCol, confidence: 'suggested', reason: 'mostly distinct, non-numeric text — plausible line-item names' } : null
  };
}

// Full analysis: given the raw sheet grid (array of arrays of cell text,
// row 0 = however the file actually starts, not assumed to be a header),
// decide whether this matches the platform's standard flat layout (exactly
// one header-like row) or a multi-block layout (a client's own multi-table
// export, like the real file this was built against, which repeats a
// "Marketing / January / February / ... / TOTAL" header row once per
// category block). Either way, returns per-column-per-block mapping with
// honest confidence levels rather than a blanket success/fail.
function analyzeMarketingBudgetGrid(grid){
  const rows = (grid || []).map(r => (r || []).map(c => (c == null ? '' : String(c))));
  const nonEmptyRows = rows.map((r, i) => ({ i, r })).filter(({ r }) => r.some(c => c.trim() !== ''));
  if (!nonEmptyRows.length){
    return { layout: 'unrecognized', headerRows: [], blocks: [], note: 'No non-empty rows found in this file.' };
  }
  const colCount = Math.max(...nonEmptyRows.map(({ r }) => r.length));
  const headerRows = nonEmptyRows.filter(({ r }) => mbuMonthMatchCount(r) >= 6).map(({ i }) => i);

  if (!headerRows.length){
    return {
      layout: 'unrecognized',
      headerRows: [],
      blocks: [],
      note: 'No row in this file contains recognizable month names (looked for at least 6 of Jan–Dec on one row) — this file doesn\'t match a month-by-month budget layout the platform can auto-detect. Every column will need manual mapping.'
    };
  }

  const layout = headerRows.length === 1 ? 'standard_flat' : 'multi_block';
  const blocks = headerRows.map((headerRowIdx, bi) => {
    const nextHeaderIdx = headerRows[bi + 1] != null ? headerRows[bi + 1] : (rows.length);
    const dataRowIdxs = nonEmptyRows.filter(({ i }) => i > headerRowIdx && i < nextHeaderIdx).map(({ i }) => i);
    const dataRows = dataRowIdxs.map(i => rows[i]);
    const headerMap = mbuMapHeaderRow(rows[headerRowIdx]);
    const suggested = mbuSuggestCategoryAndStatusColumns(dataRows, colCount);
    const mappedCols = new Set(Object.keys(headerMap).map(Number));
    const unmappedCols = [];
    for (let c = 0; c < colCount; c++){
      if (mappedCols.has(c)) continue;
      if (suggested.category && suggested.category.colIdx === c) continue;
      if (suggested.status && suggested.status.colIdx === c) continue;
      const hasAnyValue = dataRows.some(r => (r[c] || '').trim() !== '');
      if (hasAnyValue) unmappedCols.push(c);
    }
    // Subtotal/total-row detection — three independent signals, any one of
    // which flags a row. Originally just signal 1; sections 2 and 3 added
    // Round 132bx follow-on (2026-08-16) per direct bug report ("we added
    // totals to the bottom of each section, accounting-ledger style" +
    // "I don't have a way to map Total Marketing") — signal 1 alone missed
    // exactly that layout, because a ledger-style total row often has no
    // per-row annual Total on its OWN detail rows to numerically match
    // against; the total only exists as one bottom-line rollup figure.
    const totalColEntry = Object.entries(headerMap).find(([, m]) => m.key === 'total');
    const monthColEntries = Object.entries(headerMap).filter(([, m]) => m.key !== 'total');
    let likelySubtotalRowIdxs = [];
    if (dataRows.length > 1){
      const totalColIdx = totalColEntry ? Number(totalColEntry[0]) : null;
      const rowTotal = r => (totalColIdx != null ? mbuNumberOrNull(r[totalColIdx]) : null);
      const rowMonthSum = r => monthColEntries.reduce((s, [idx]) => s + (mbuNumberOrNull(r[Number(idx)]) || 0), 0);
      const rowHasAnyMonthValue = r => monthColEntries.some(([idx]) => mbuNumberOrNull(r[Number(idx)]) != null);

      // Signal 1 (label match) runs FIRST and on purpose, not last: a row
      // whose category text plainly says this is a total ("Total
      // Consulting Services", "TOTAL MARKETING EXECUTION") is flagged
      // immediately, independent of what the numbers do. This matters for
      // the numeric signals below, not just as its own check — a real
      // client file (this platform's own reference file among them) can
      // have a whole-file grand-total row swept into the last block
      // alongside that block's own section subtotal, and that grand-total
      // row's own Total-column figure is frequently a separate, inflated,
      // double-counted number (summing both subtotal AND every detail row
      // beneath it) that doesn't match anything -- included in the "sum of
      // every other row" arithmetic below, it silently corrupts detection
      // of the block's REAL section subtotal too. Removing label-matched
      // rows from that arithmetic up front avoids this.
      const categoryColIdx = suggested.category ? suggested.category.colIdx : null;
      if (categoryColIdx != null){
        dataRowIdxs.forEach((ri, di) => {
          if (mbuIsTotalLikeCategoryLabel((dataRows[di][categoryColIdx] || '').trim())) likelySubtotalRowIdxs.push(ri);
        });
      }

      // Signal 2: this row's own Total-column value equals the sum of
      // every OTHER not-already-flagged row's own Total-column value in
      // this block — importing it alongside its own detail rows would
      // double-count. Deliberately excludes label-matched rows (signal 1)
      // from both the candidate set and the sum, so a stray grand-total row
      // swept into this block can't throw off detection of this block's
      // own real subtotal (see comment above).
      //
      // Flags AT MOST ONE row per block, never all matches: when a section
      // has exactly one real line item, that item's own total is
      // necessarily IDENTICAL to the section's rollup total (nothing else
      // to sum), so both rows satisfy "my total equals the sum of the
      // other" simultaneously and the equation alone can't tell them apart
      // — found against this account's own real file, where "Photo/Video -
      // 3% of total" and its single line item "Photo Shoots" share the
      // exact same $270,000 figure, and flagging both dropped the real
      // line item entirely (a silent $270k shortfall in the confirmed
      // budget, only caught by summing the review screen against the
      // file's own stated grand total). Ties resolve to the earliest row,
      // matching every section in this file, where the rollup consistently
      // appears first, right after the header, before its detail rows.
      if (totalColIdx != null){
        const candidates = dataRowIdxs.filter(ri => !likelySubtotalRowIdxs.includes(ri));
        const totals = candidates.map(ri => ({ rowIdx: ri, total: rowTotal(dataRows[dataRowIdxs.indexOf(ri)]) }));
        const grand = totals.reduce((s, t) => s + (t.total || 0), 0);
        const matches = totals.filter(t => t.total != null && t.total > 0 && Math.abs((grand - t.total) - t.total) < 1);
        if (matches.length) likelySubtotalRowIdxs.push(matches[0].rowIdx);
      }

      // Signal 3 — accounting-ledger layout: individual line items carry
      // monthly figures but no per-row annual Total of their own (so signal
      // 2 has nothing to match against); the real total is a single
      // bottom-line annual figure with blank month cells. A row with a
      // Total value, no monthly values of its own, sitting alongside other
      // (not-already-flagged) rows that DO have monthly values, whose Total
      // roughly equals the sum of every other row's monthly figures, is a
      // rollup — not a line item.
      if (totalColIdx != null && monthColEntries.length){
        dataRowIdxs.forEach((ri, di) => {
          if (likelySubtotalRowIdxs.includes(ri)) return;
          const r = dataRows[di];
          const t = rowTotal(r);
          if (t == null || t <= 0 || rowHasAnyMonthValue(r)) return;
          const othersMonthSum = dataRowIdxs.reduce((s, ri2, di2) => {
            if (ri2 === ri || likelySubtotalRowIdxs.includes(ri2)) return s;
            return s + rowMonthSum(dataRows[di2]);
          }, 0);
          if (othersMonthSum > 0 && Math.abs(othersMonthSum - t) < 1) likelySubtotalRowIdxs.push(ri);
        });
      }
    }

    return {
      headerRowIdx,
      dataRowIdxs,
      headerText: rows[headerRowIdx],
      monthAndTotalMap: headerMap,
      suggestedCategoryCol: suggested.category,
      suggestedStatusCol: suggested.status,
      unmappedColumns: unmappedCols,
      likelySubtotalRowIdxs,
      rowCount: dataRows.length
    };
  });

  const monthsFound = new Set();
  blocks.forEach(b => Object.values(b.monthAndTotalMap).forEach(m => { if (m.key !== 'total') monthsFound.add(m.key); }));
  const allAutoConfident = layout === 'standard_flat'
    && blocks[0].unmappedColumns.length === 0
    && blocks[0].suggestedCategoryCol && blocks[0].suggestedStatusCol
    && monthsFound.size === 12;

  // Real, computed grand-total detection, same spirit as the per-block
  // subtotal check above but across every block: a file with no header row
  // of its own (a trailing "TOTAL MARKETING EXECUTION"-style row) gets
  // swept into whichever block happens to be last, since block boundaries
  // are only drawn at header-like rows. If a row's Total-column value
  // equals the sum of every block's own best-known total (its detected
  // subtotal row if there is exactly one, else the sum of that block's raw
  // row totals), it's very likely an account-wide grand total, not a line
  // item belonging to the last block — flagged for exclusion, not silently
  // imported as a bogus "category."
  const blockTotals = blocks.map(b => {
    const totalEntry = Object.entries(b.monthAndTotalMap).find(([, m]) => m.key === 'total');
    if (!totalEntry) return null;
    const totalColIdx = Number(totalEntry[0]);
    const dataRows = b.dataRowIdxs.map(ri => rows[ri]);
    if (b.likelySubtotalRowIdxs.length === 1){
      const ri = b.dataRowIdxs.indexOf(b.likelySubtotalRowIdxs[0]);
      return mbuNumberOrNull(dataRows[ri][totalColIdx]);
    }
    const vals = dataRows.map(r => mbuNumberOrNull(r[totalColIdx])).filter(v => v != null);
    return vals.length ? vals.reduce((s, v) => s + v, 0) / (b.likelySubtotalRowIdxs.length ? 2 : 1) : null;
  }).filter(v => v != null);
  const likelyGrandTotalRowIdxs = [];
  if (blockTotals.length > 1){
    const grandCandidate = blockTotals.reduce((s, v) => s + v, 0);
    const lastBlock = blocks[blocks.length - 1];
    const totalEntry = Object.entries(lastBlock.monthAndTotalMap).find(([, m]) => m.key === 'total');
    if (totalEntry){
      const totalColIdx = Number(totalEntry[0]);
      lastBlock.dataRowIdxs.forEach(ri => {
        if (lastBlock.likelySubtotalRowIdxs.includes(ri)) return;
        const v = mbuNumberOrNull(rows[ri][totalColIdx]);
        if (v != null && Math.abs(v - grandCandidate) < 1) likelyGrandTotalRowIdxs.push(ri);
      });
    }
  }

  return {
    layout,
    headerRows,
    blocks,
    monthsRecognized: Array.from(monthsFound),
    likelyGrandTotalRowIdxs,
    status: allAutoConfident ? 'mapped' : 'needs_mapping',
    note: layout === 'multi_block'
      ? `Found ${headerRows.length} separate header-like rows (a repeating multi-table layout, not one flat table) — month and total columns are auto-recognized within each block from their own header text, but the category-name and Working/Non-Working columns aren't reliably labeled anywhere in this file and need manual confirmation per block.${likelyGrandTotalRowIdxs.length ? ` Row(s) ${likelyGrandTotalRowIdxs.map(i => i + 1).join(', ')} look like an account-wide grand total swept into the last block, not a real line item — review before confirming.` : ''}`
      : (allAutoConfident
        ? 'Single header row found; category, status, all 12 months, and total all auto-recognized with high confidence.'
        : 'Single header row found, but not every target field could be confidently auto-mapped — review the suggested/unmapped columns below.')
  };
}

// Financial tracking (Deliverable #11), per cxmedia-deliverable-status-vs-
// brief.md's own framing: distinct from Media Plan's budget ALLOCATION
// (planning, already built) and from account.html's payment-methods mock
// (how a client would pay, still a genuine "no real processor" mock below)
// — this is real tracking of what CX Experiences has actually billed this
// account, and what's been paid, as an actual persisted ledger rather than
// account.html's previous hardcoded three-row invoice list. No real payment
// processor is wired up here (marking an invoice 'paid' is a real database
// record of a real business fact — an invoice got paid — not an instruction
// to move money; that stays firmly out of scope for this build, same as
// the account.html card explicitly still being a mock for card/ACH capture).
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS invoices (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    invoiceNumber TEXT NOT NULL,
    description TEXT,
    amount REAL NOT NULL,
    status TEXT DEFAULT 'sent',
    issuedDate TEXT,
    dueDate TEXT,
    paidDate TEXT,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    updatedAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId)
  );
`);
const INVOICE_STATUSES = ['draft', 'sent', 'paid', 'overdue'];

// Copy Library — per direct instruction: "user should be able to edit copy
// and save copy to library for future use." One flat table per account,
// not scoped to a single campaign (campaignId is stored for context/filter
// but a saved snippet is meant to be reusable across campaigns), same
// pattern as print_specs_custom's account-scoped saves.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS copy_library (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    campaignId TEXT,
    channel TEXT,
    label TEXT,
    text TEXT NOT NULL,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId)
  );
`);

// Round 59 — CRM integration lead capture (Deliverable 13). Not tied to an
// accountId: a lead is captured at the free assessment's contact step,
// before any account exists — accountId only comes into being later, if
// this lead converts through onboarding.html. hubspotStatus is one of
// 'not_configured' (no HUBSPOT_ACCESS_TOKEN set — the default today),
// 'synced' (HubSpot upsert succeeded, hubspotContactId set), or 'error'
// (HubSpot was configured but the call failed — hubspotError set). A lead
// is never lost because HubSpot isn't reachable or isn't set up yet; this
// table is always the durable record, HubSpot sync is best-effort on top.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS leads (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    email TEXT NOT NULL,
    firstName TEXT,
    lastName TEXT,
    phoneCountry TEXT,
    phone TEXT,
    company TEXT,
    industry TEXT,
    footprint TEXT,
    buyerType TEXT,
    address TEXT,
    engagementModel TEXT,
    quadrant TEXT,
    mediaPct REAL,
    cxPct REAL,
    consent INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
    source TEXT,
    hubspotStatus TEXT DEFAULT 'pending',
    hubspotContactId TEXT,
    hubspotError TEXT,
    createdAt TEXT,
    assessmentDataJson TEXT
  );
`);
// Round 126 (2026-08-08) — assessmentDataJson holds the full structured
// assessment profile (org style, maturity levels, loop effectiveness,
// function-group ratings, assessed stages, measured stage scores,
// opportunity size, audience/wealth) as one JSON blob — the durable local
// audit trail for everything that also gets flattened into named HubSpot
// properties by buildHubspotProperties() below. Added via a plain ALTER
// (not baked into the CREATE TABLE above) so this upgrades an existing
// cxmedia.db in place without needing a migration script — SQLite's ALTER
// TABLE ADD COLUMN is safe to attempt unconditionally and this swallows
// the "duplicate column" error on every run after the first.
try { db.exec(`ALTER TABLE leads ADD COLUMN assessmentDataJson TEXT`); } catch (e) { /* already exists */ }

// Round 60 — session tokens (Deliverable 14/auth). One row per active
// login; deleted on logout or lazily on expiry (see authenticate() below).
// Not a JWT — a real opaque, server-checked token, so revoking one is a
// single DELETE rather than needing a blocklist the way a stateless JWT
// would.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS sessions (
    token TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    expiresAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId)
  );
`);
// Added 2026-08-18 — real per-person login (registration rebuild). Nullable
// on purpose: existing account-level access-code sessions (createSession()
// called with no memberId) keep working unchanged everywhere that only
// checks session.accountId (requireAccount() below never looks at
// memberId), while a session created by the new POST /api/auth/login-user
// carries the specific team_members.id that logged in, so a route that
// cares WHICH person is acting (not just which account) has that to read.
ensureColumn('sessions', 'memberId', 'TEXT');

// Round 61 — Print Ad Specs (Print channel of the omni-channel creative
// selection tree, cxmedia-creative-specs-tree.json). Per direct instruction:
// the master Publication/Ad-Format/dimensions catalog Todd supplied
// (Print_Advertising_Specs_Master_v4.xlsx) is shared reference data, common
// to every account, so it lives as a static constant below rather than a
// DB table — there is nothing account-specific to persist for it. What IS
// account-specific is a team adding a publication the master catalog is
// missing, so THAT gets a real table, scoped by accountId exactly like
// team_members above.
db.exec(`
  CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS print_specs_custom (
    id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
    accountId TEXT NOT NULL,
    publication TEXT NOT NULL,
    publisher TEXT,
    mediumType TEXT NOT NULL,
    adFormat TEXT NOT NULL,
    trimWidthIn REAL,
    trimHeightIn REAL,
    bleedWidthIn TEXT,
    bleedHeightIn TEXT,
    liveWidthIn REAL,
    liveHeightIn REAL,
    colorProfile TEXT,
    dpi TEXT,
    fileFormat TEXT,
    inkLimit TEXT,
    richBlack TEXT,
    notes TEXT,
    createdAt TEXT NOT NULL,
    FOREIGN KEY (accountId) REFERENCES accounts(accountId)
  );
`);

// 2026-08-21 fix -- sitewide camelCase column casing repair.
//
// WHY THIS EXISTS: sql-translate.js quotes known camelCase identifiers
// (e.g. "accountId") so Postgres preserves their case; anything NOT quoted
// gets folded to all-lowercase by Postgres automatically. A column created
// via CREATE TABLE / ensureColumn BEFORE that identifier existed in
// schema-identifiers.json was created unquoted -- so its real, permanent
// physical name in the live database is all-lowercase (e.g. "accountid",
// "passwordhash"), even though every query in this file references it
// quoted/camelCase. That mismatch doesn't fail loudly -- Postgres either
// throws "column ... does not exist" (a hard 500, e.g. what broke
// POST /api/auth/login-user on 2026-08-21) or, in queries that don't
// explicitly reference the mismatched column, just silently returns
// JavaScript's `undefined` for it (e.g. member.passwordHash reading as
// undefined forever, making password verification impossible while
// looking exactly like a normal "wrong password" response -- this is very
// likely the real root cause of the standing "incorrect email or
// password" reports, more fundamental than the duplicate-row theory).
//
// FIX: for every camelCase column this codebase declares anywhere (built
// from every ensureColumn() call and every CREATE TABLE column list),
// attempt to rename the all-lowercase-folded form to the correct quoted
// camelCase form. This is idempotent and safe by construction, same
// try/catch convention as ensureColumn() above:
//   - Column already correctly camelCase -> the lowercase RENAME target
//     doesn't exist -> Postgres throws -> caught, no-op.
//   - Column still legacy-lowercase -> renamed in place (data preserved;
//     RENAME COLUMN never touches data, and Postgres updates any foreign
//     key/index that references it automatically).
//   - Column doesn't exist on this table at all -> caught, no-op.
const LEGACY_CASING_COLUMNS = [

// 2026-08-21, later same day — this used to run automatically at module
// load (`fixLegacyColumnCasing();` right here, on every cold start), doing
// up to 350 sequential blocking ALTER TABLE round-trips through the
// synchronous worker-thread bridge before the server could handle its
// first request. That's what was SUPPOSED to only cost a few seconds on a
// mostly-already-fixed database, but under real network latency to
// Supabase it was marginal against Vercel's 30s function timeout and
// started failing outright — every endpoint (`login-user`, `logout`,
// `GET /api/accounts/:id`) came back `INTERNAL_FUNCTION_INVOCATION_FAILED`
// because the COLD START itself never finished within 30s, not because of
// any actual query error. This was a self-inflicted regression: the fix
// for the casing bug became a worse outage than the casing bug.
//
// Fixed by removing the automatic run entirely and turning this into an
// on-demand admin action instead (`POST /api/admin/fix-legacy-casing`,
// below), gated by the same ADMIN_API_TOKEN pattern already used for
// `GET /api/ops/integration-status`. It also does a single bulk
// information_schema lookup first to find out which columns actually
// still need renaming, so a re-run after a mostly-successful prior run is
// fast (near-zero remaining work) instead of repeating all 350 checks
// every time.
function fixLegacyColumnCasing(){
  // 2026-08-21 -- this used to swallow every error identically, which made
  // it impossible to tell "nothing to fix" apart from "this is silently
  // failing" from the Vercel logs alone. Now: count real renames, and log
  // (don't just eat) any error that ISN'T Postgres's normal "column does
  // not exist" (code 42703, or the equivalent local-SQLite message) --
  // that's the expected/harmless outcome for a column that's already
  // correctly cased or doesn't exist on that table; anything else (a
  // permissions error, a lock, a typo) is worth seeing.
  //
  // Bulk pre-check: one query against information_schema.columns for
  // every table this list touches, instead of finding out column-by-column
  // via 350 individual failed-ALTER round trips. Anything not present in
  // this result under its LOWERCASE name has already been fixed (or never
  // existed) and needs no ALTER at all.
  const tables = Array.from(new Set(LEGACY_CASING_COLUMNS.map(([t]) => t)));
  let existing = new Set();
  try {
    const placeholders = tables.map(() => '?').join(',');
    const rows = db.prepare(
      `SELECT table_name, column_name FROM information_schema.columns WHERE table_schema = 'public' AND table_name IN (${placeholders})`
    ).all(...tables);
    existing = new Set(rows.map((r) => `${r.table_name}.${r.column_name}`));
  } catch (e) {
    // If the bulk check itself fails for some reason, fall back to
    // attempting every rename directly below rather than silently doing
    // nothing — slower, but still correct.
    console.error('[fixLegacyColumnCasing] bulk information_schema check failed, falling back to per-column attempts:', (e && e.message) || e);
    existing = null;
  }

  let renamed = 0, alreadyOk = 0, unexpectedErrors = 0, droppedDuplicates = 0;
  const errorDetails = [];
  for (const [table, col] of LEGACY_CASING_COLUMNS){
    const lower = col.toLowerCase();
    if (lower === col) continue; // no casing to fix
    if (existing && !existing.has(`${table}.${lower}`)){
      alreadyOk++; // confirmed via the bulk check — nothing to do, no round trip spent
      continue;
    }
    try {
      db.exec('ALTER TABLE ' + table + ' RENAME COLUMN ' + lower + ' TO ' + col);
      renamed++;
    } catch (e) {
      const msg = (e && e.message) || '';
      const isExpected = e && (e.code === '42703' || /does not exist/i.test(msg) || /no such column/i.test(msg));
      // 2026-08-21, later still — Postgres 42701 "column already exists"
      // means the correctly-cased column was already added separately
      // (via ensureColumn()'s ADD COLUMN, before this table's rename ever
      // ran), so BOTH the old lowercase and the new camelCase column now
      // exist side by side. Confirmed with Todd directly: the app only
      // reads/writes the camelCase one, this environment's data is test
      // data expected to be wiped before the next phase anyway, and he
      // explicitly asked (2026-08-21) to have the leftover lowercase
      // duplicate dropped rather than left as unused clutter — so a 42701
      // here drops the old column instead of just being logged.
      const isDuplicate = e && e.code === '42701';
      if (isExpected) {
        alreadyOk++;
      } else if (isDuplicate) {
        try {
          db.exec('ALTER TABLE ' + table + ' DROP COLUMN ' + lower);
          droppedDuplicates++;
        } catch (dropErr) {
          unexpectedErrors++;
          const dropMsg = (dropErr && dropErr.message) || '';
          if (errorDetails.length < 25){
            errorDetails.push({ table, column: lower, targetColumn: col, code: (dropErr && dropErr.code) || null, message: `rename hit duplicate (42701), then DROP COLUMN also failed: ${dropMsg}` });
          }
          console.error(`[fixLegacyColumnCasing] duplicate column ${table}.${lower} — DROP also failed:`, dropMsg);
        }
      } else {
        unexpectedErrors++;
        // 2026-08-21, later still — return the actual error alongside the
        // counts (not just log it) so diagnosing a nonzero
        // unexpectedErrors doesn't require a trip to the Vercel log
        // viewer. Capped at 25 entries — this list is meant to be read by
        // a person, not machine-parsed in bulk.
        if (errorDetails.length < 25){
          errorDetails.push({ table, column: lower, targetColumn: col, code: (e && e.code) || null, message: msg });
        }
        console.error(`[fixLegacyColumnCasing] unexpected error renaming ${table}.${lower} -> ${col}:`, msg);
      }
    }
  }
  const summary = { renamed, alreadyOk, droppedDuplicates, unexpectedErrors, totalConsidered: LEGACY_CASING_COLUMNS.length, errorDetails };
  console.log(`[fixLegacyColumnCasing] done: ${renamed} column(s) renamed, ${alreadyOk} already correct/absent, ${droppedDuplicates} duplicate(s) dropped, ${unexpectedErrors} unexpected error(s)`);
  return summary;
}
// NOTE: deliberately NOT auto-run at module load anymore — see the comment
// above. Trigger it on demand via POST /api/admin/fix-legacy-casing.

// Footprint → region code, matching account.html/portal.html's existing
// sample ID convention (e.g. "CXM-NAT-2026-483").
const REGION_CODE = { Local: 'LOC', National: 'NAT', Franchise: 'FRN' };

function generateAccountId(footprint){
  const code = REGION_CODE[footprint] || 'NAT';
  const year = new Date().getFullYear();
  // 2026-08-20 fix — this was 3 digits (only 900 possible values per
  // footprint+year combination), which is exactly the collision risk the
  // comment on generateId() below already warned about for a different ID
  // generator. It wasn't hypothetical: a live Vercel log showed
  // POST /api/accounts failing with a real Postgres "duplicate key value
  // violates unique constraint" error on the accounts table's primary key,
  // almost certainly this. Widened to 6 digits (900,000 possible values) to
  // make a collision far less likely — the actual guarantee against it is
  // the catch-and-retry loop around the INSERT in POST /api/accounts below,
  // not the size of this number alone.
  const suffix = String(Math.floor(Math.random() * 900000) + 100000); // 6 digits
  return `CXM-${code}-${year}-${suffix}`;
}

// 2026-08-20 — used by POST /api/accounts' retry loop (see below) to tell
// "this accountId already exists, generate a new one and try again" apart
// from every other kind of database error, which should still fail the
// request normally. Postgres reports a unique-violation as error code
// '23505' (set on pg-sync-bridge.js's thrown Error, sourced from the `pg`
// driver); local SQLite (node:sqlite's DatabaseSync) has no such code and
// instead throws a plain Error whose message contains "UNIQUE constraint
// failed" — checking both keeps this correct in local dev and production.
function isDuplicateKeyError(e){
  return (e && e.code === '23505') || /UNIQUE constraint failed/i.test((e && e.message) || '');
}

// A per-process monotonic counter, not just Date.now() + random, because
// Round 132bx (cont'd, 2nd pass)'s auto-import now bulk-inserts many line
// items (one per category × month) synchronously in a single request —
// easily hundreds of generateId() calls within the same millisecond, where
// the 900-value random suffix alone collided often enough to throw a real
// UNIQUE constraint error under the multi-block test file this was caught
// against. The counter guarantees uniqueness within this process
// regardless of call volume or timing; the timestamp/random parts are kept
// so IDs still sort roughly chronologically and stay hard to guess.
let generateIdCounter = 0;
function generateId(prefix){
  generateIdCounter = (generateIdCounter + 1) % 1e9;
  return `${prefix}-${Date.now().toString(36)}-${Math.floor(Math.random() * 900 + 100)}-${generateIdCounter.toString(36)}`;
}

// Round 132c17 (2026-08-17), per direct instruction: "the campaign id
// includes a 3 letter code for each partner defined during setup, three
// letter product code -last two of start year -last two of month year.
// Atlas would be AOV-POL (Polar)-27-04". Letters-only, uppercased, capped
// at 3 chars each; year/month come from the campaign's own startDate (falls
// back to today if no start date is on file yet, since a campaign can exist
// before its dates are locked in). Returns null (not a fabricated code) when
// the account has no partnerCode on file yet — Company Profile gates this,
// same "real or explicitly missing" convention as everywhere else in this
// file, rather than guessing a code from the company name.
function threeLetterCode(str){
  const clean = String(str || '').replace(/[^A-Za-z]/g, '').toUpperCase();
  return clean ? clean.slice(0, 3) : '';
}
function generateCampaignCode(accountId, partnerCode, productCode, startDate){
  const cleanPartner = threeLetterCode(partnerCode);
  const cleanProduct = threeLetterCode(productCode);
  if (!cleanPartner || !cleanProduct) return null;
  const d = startDate ? new Date(startDate) : new Date();
  const validDate = !isNaN(d.getTime()) ? d : new Date();
  const yy = String(validDate.getFullYear()).slice(-2);
  const mm = String(validDate.getMonth() + 1).padStart(2, '0');
  const base = `${cleanPartner}-${cleanProduct}-${yy}-${mm}`;
  // Same partner+product+month can recur across multiple campaigns (e.g.
  // two Polar campaigns both launching in April) — Campaign IDs must be
  // unique (per direct instruction), so append a disambiguating suffix
  // rather than silently colliding.
  let code = base;
  let n = 2;
  while (db.prepare('SELECT id FROM campaigns WHERE campaignCode = ?').get(code)){
    code = `${base}-${n}`;
    n += 1;
  }
  return code;
}

// Global print specs catalog — transcribed from Todd's
// Print_Advertising_Specs_Master_v4.xlsx ("Submission Guidelines" +
// "All Media Print Specs" sheets). Derived fields (colorProfile, dpi,
// fileFormat, inkLimit, richBlack, gutterSafety) are computed once here from
// the Submission Guidelines sheet's global rules rather than repeated by
// hand on every row, so there's one place to update if those house rules
// change. Dimensions are exactly as given in the source workbook (inches);
// 'N/A' bleed means that ad format has no bleed (page-fit newsprint units,
// or a magazine's explicitly non-bleed unit).
function buildPrintSpec(row){
  const isMagazine = row.mediumType === 'Magazine';
  return {
    id: `PRINT-GLOBAL-${row.publication.replace(/\s+/g, '').toUpperCase()}-${row.adFormat.replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/gi, '').toUpperCase()}`,
    publication: row.publication,
    publisher: row.publisher,
    mediumType: row.mediumType,
    adFormat: row.adFormat,
    trimWidthIn: row.trimWidthIn,
    trimHeightIn: row.trimHeightIn,
    bleedWidthIn: row.bleedWidthIn,
    bleedHeightIn: row.bleedHeightIn,
    liveWidthIn: row.liveWidthIn,
    liveHeightIn: row.liveHeightIn,
    colorProfile: isMagazine ? 'SWOP 3 / GRACoL 2013 (coated magazine stock)' : 'SNAP (coldset newsprint)',
    dpi: isMagazine ? '300 DPI minimum @ 100% size' : '200 DPI acceptable (300 DPI preferred) @ 100% size',
    fileFormat: 'PDF/X-1a or PDF/X-4 — CMYK only, all fonts embedded, crop marks outside bleed',
    inkLimit: isMagazine ? 'Max 300% TAC (Total Area Coverage)' : 'Max 220%-240% TAC (Total Area Coverage)',
    richBlack: '60% C / 40% M / 30% Y / 100% K for solid black backgrounds/large shapes; small body text must be 100% K only',
    gutterSafety: /spread/i.test(row.adFormat) ? '0.25-0.375 in (6-10mm) clearance from spine fold — keep live text/logos out of this margin' : null,
    notes: row.notes,
    source: 'global',
  };
}

// Round 62 — Direct Mail specs, added from Todd's updated
// Print_Advertising_and_Direct_Mail_Master_v5.xlsx ("Direct Mail Specs
// (USPS Max)" sheet). Per direct instruction, a Direct Mail project's two
// selectable fields are Size (the named format) and USPS Postal Class —
// everything else (binding/fold style, trim/bleed/live, paper stock, max
// weight, addressing-zone clearance, USPS aspect-ratio-discount rule, and
// the discount/production notes column) fills in automatically and gets
// sent to the creative team, same "select the few things that vary, derive
// the rest" pattern as buildPrintSpec() above.
function buildDirectMailSpec(row){
  return {
    id: `DM-GLOBAL-${row.formatName.replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/gi, '').toUpperCase()}`,
    formatName: row.formatName,
    postalClass: row.postalClass,
    bindingFoldStyle: row.bindingFoldStyle,
    trimWidthIn: row.trimWidthIn,
    trimHeightIn: row.trimHeightIn,
    bleedWidthIn: row.bleedWidthIn,
    bleedHeightIn: row.bleedHeightIn,
    liveWidthIn: row.liveWidthIn,
    liveHeightIn: row.liveHeightIn,
    paperStock: row.paperStock,
    maxWeightOz: row.maxWeightOz,
    colorProfile: 'SWOP 3 / GRACoL 2013 (coated stock, sheetfed — same profile as Magazines)',
    dpi: '300 DPI minimum @ 100% size',
    fileFormat: 'PDF/X-1a or PDF/X-4 — CMYK only, all fonts embedded, crop marks outside bleed',
    inkLimit: 'Max 300% TAC (Total Area Coverage)',
    richBlack: '60% C / 40% M / 30% Y / 100% K for solid black backgrounds/large shapes; small body text must be 100% K only',
    addressingZone: '4.00" W x 2.25" H clearance zone, lower right corner — keep completely clear of graphics/text for the IMB barcode & address spray',
    aspectRatioRule: /Letter/.test(row.postalClass) ? 'Width ÷ height must fall between 1.3 and 2.5 to qualify for the USPS Automation Letter rate.' : null,
    notes: row.notes,
    source: 'global',
  };
}

// Round 63 — Out-of-Home / Digital Out-of-Home specs, from Todd's
// Omnichannel_Advertising_Specs_Master.xlsx ("OOH & DOOH Specs" sheet). Per
// direct instruction, the "Outdoor" channel in the creative selection tree
// is renamed Out-of-Home (OOH) — this catalog is what actually populates it.
// The Publication Specs / Direct Mail Specs sheets in this same workbook are
// a smaller subset of what rounds 61-62 already transcribed in full (same
// values, just fewer rows), so GLOBAL_PRINT_SPECS/GLOBAL_DIRECT_MAIL_SPECS
// above are left as the fuller, already-verified data rather than being
// overwritten with a subset.
//
// Two selectable fields, same "pick the few things that vary, derive the
// rest" pattern as Print/Direct Mail: OOH Category (Billboards, Street
// Furniture, Transit, Place-Based), then Media Type/Format within that
// category. Resolution rule, bleed/pocket requirement, and the DOOH file
// format/color space/frame rate spec are all derived from the Submission
// Guidelines sheet's house rules rather than repeated per row — DOOH fields
// only apply when a format actually has a digital resolution (some OOH
// formats, e.g. Large Bulletin or Bus King Panel, are static/vinyl only).
function buildOohSpec(row){
  const isLargeFormat = row.category === 'Billboards';
  const resolutionRule = isLargeFormat
    ? '18-25 DPI @ 100% scale (large-format viewing distance) — files are often built at 300 DPI at 1/12th scale, i.e. 1 inch = 1 foot'
    : '80-100 DPI @ 100% scale (eye-level viewing requires higher resolution than highway/large-format units)';
  const isStatic = /Static|Vinyl|Paper|Hand-Painted/i.test(row.mediumSupport);
  const bleedNote = isStatic ? '0.125" standard bleed for paper/poster stock; 3"-6" vinyl pocket wraps required for static vinyl bulletins' : null;
  const isDigitalCapable = row.doohResolution && row.doohResolution !== 'N/A';
  return {
    id: `OOH-GLOBAL-${row.category.replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/gi, '').toUpperCase()}-${row.formatName.replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/gi, '').toUpperCase()}`,
    category: row.category,
    formatName: row.formatName,
    mediumSupport: row.mediumSupport,
    displayDimensions: row.displayDimensions,
    doohResolution: row.doohResolution,
    aspectRatio: row.aspectRatio,
    resolutionRule,
    bleedNote,
    fileFormat: isDigitalCapable ? 'Static DOOH: JPEG/PNG-24 (sRGB). Motion DOOH: MP4/MOV (H.264, no audio track).' : (isStatic ? 'Static print production file (press-ready PDF or high-res JPEG/TIFF per vendor spec)' : null),
    colorSpace: isDigitalCapable ? 'sRGB IEC61966-2.1 — do NOT use CMYK for digital boards' : null,
    frameRate: isDigitalCapable ? '25-30 fps max; no flashing faster than 3 Hz per US DOT standards' : null,
    notes: row.notes,
    source: 'global',
  };
}

// across every account (the articles themselves don't vary by client), but

// that account's own active team roster (see the /daily-brief endpoint
// below) — real, per-account personalization on top of shared content, not
// per-account content generation.

// itself makes about the Media Loop, which is this build's own taxonomy.

// manual editorial pass, same as this build's other reference-data tables;
// there's no live feed pulling new articles in automatically.

// ---------- Round 60 — authentication (Deliverable 14) ----------
//
// Access codes are the "password" here — issued once, server-generated
// (never client-chosen, so there's no weak-password problem to guard
// against), shown to the client exactly once at account-creation time, and
// never stored or logged in plaintext anywhere after that. Hashed with
// Node's built-in scrypt (a real, slow, salted KDF — not a bare SHA hash),
// same "no npm dependency" constraint as the rest of this file.

// Base32-ish alphabet with ambiguous characters (0/O, 1/I/L) removed, so a
// human reading this off a screen to type into a login field doesn't hit
// an character they can't tell apart. 10 characters ~= 48 bits of entropy —
// plenty for a locally-run demo; a production build behind a real identity
// provider wouldn't need this scheme at all (see this file's header
// comment).
const ACCESS_CODE_ALPHABET = 'ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTUVWXYZ23456789';
function generateAccessCode(){
  let code = '';
  const bytes = crypto.randomBytes(10);
  for (let i = 0; i < 10; i++) code += ACCESS_CODE_ALPHABET[bytes[i] % ACCESS_CODE_ALPHABET.length];
  return `${code.slice(0, 5)}-${code.slice(5)}`; // e.g. "7F3KQ-9XM2P", easier to read/type in two halves
}

function hashAccessCode(code, salt){
  const useSalt = salt || crypto.randomBytes(16).toString('hex');
  const hash = crypto.scryptSync(code, useSalt, 64).toString('hex');
  return { hash, salt: useSalt };
}

function verifyAccessCode(code, hash, salt){
  if (!hash || !salt) return false;
  const check = crypto.scryptSync(code, salt, 64).toString('hex');
  // Fixed-time compare — a plain `===` on hash strings leaks timing
  // information about where the first mismatched character is.
  const a = Buffer.from(check, 'hex');
  const b = Buffer.from(hash, 'hex');
  return a.length === b.length && crypto.timingSafeEqual(a, b);
}

const SESSION_LIFETIME_MS = 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000; // 7 days

function createSession(accountId, memberId){
  const token = crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('hex');
  const now = new Date();
  const expiresAt = new Date(now.getTime() + SESSION_LIFETIME_MS).toISOString();
  db.prepare('INSERT INTO sessions (token, accountId, memberId, createdAt, expiresAt) VALUES (?,?,?,?,?)')
    .run(token, accountId, memberId || null, now.toISOString(), expiresAt);
  return { token, expiresAt };
}

// Added 2026-08-20 — see trusted_devices' own comment above. 30 days,
// intentionally longer than a session (7 days) — the device trust and the
// session it's paired with expire independently; a returning visitor with
// an expired session but a still-trusted device gets a fresh session
// without a new MFA code, but does still need to re-enter their password
// (this only skips the SECOND factor, never the first).
const DEVICE_TRUST_LIFETIME_MS = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000; // 30 days

function createTrustedDevice(memberId){
  const token = crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('hex');
  const now = new Date();
  const expiresAt = new Date(now.getTime() + DEVICE_TRUST_LIFETIME_MS).toISOString();
  db.prepare('INSERT INTO trusted_devices (token, memberId, createdAt, expiresAt, lastUsedAt) VALUES (?,?,?,?,?)')
    .run(token, memberId, now.toISOString(), expiresAt, now.toISOString());
  return { token, expiresAt };
}

// Returns true (and refreshes lastUsedAt) only for a token that exists,
// hasn't expired, and belongs to THIS member — a device trusted for one
// person's login doesn't let it skip MFA for a different person's,
// including a different member on a shared account.
function checkTrustedDevice(memberId, token){
  if (!token) return false;
  const row = db.prepare('SELECT * FROM trusted_devices WHERE token = ? AND memberId = ?').get(token, memberId);
  if (!row) return false;
  if (new Date(row.expiresAt).getTime() < Date.now()){
    db.prepare('DELETE FROM trusted_devices WHERE token = ?').run(token);
    return false;
  }
  db.prepare('UPDATE trusted_devices SET lastUsedAt = ? WHERE token = ?').run(new Date().toISOString(), token);
  return true;
}

// Added 2026-08-18 — a system-issued temporary password for a freshly
// registered admin/CMO user, per direct instruction: real, memorable
// username+password rather than the access-code model, with a temp
// password on first issuance that forces a change on first login. Same
// alphabet/shape convention as generateAccessCode() below (no ambiguous
// 0/O/1/I/l characters), just a distinct name so its call sites read
// unambiguously as "temp password", not "access code".
// Added 2026-08-18 — best-effort client IP for the legal-acceptance audit
// trail. x-forwarded-for is checked first since this runs behind Vercel's
// edge network in production (the direct socket address there is Vercel's
// proxy, not the visitor); falls back to the raw socket address for local
// dev, where there's no proxy in front of it. x-forwarded-for can carry a
// comma-separated chain (client, proxy1, proxy2, ...) — the first entry is
// the original client.
function getClientIp(req){
  const forwarded = req.headers['x-forwarded-for'];
  if (forwarded) return forwarded.split(',')[0].trim();
  return (req.socket && req.socket.remoteAddress) || null;
}

// Added 2026-08-18 — one row per accepted document, per direct instruction
// that every legal-acceptance instance (T&Cs/pricing disclosures at
// registration, NDA if opted into) gets its own persisted, timestamped,
// identified record. Called from POST /api/auth/register-admin below.
function recordLegalAcceptance(req, { accountId, memberId, documentType, documentVersion, name, email, method }){
  db.prepare(`INSERT INTO legal_acceptances
    (id, accountId, memberId, documentType, documentVersion, acceptedAt, acceptedByName, acceptedByEmail, ipAddress, userAgent, acceptanceMethod)
    VALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?)`)
    .run(
      generateId('LEGAL'), accountId, memberId || null, documentType, documentVersion,
      new Date().toISOString(), name || null, email || null,
      getClientIp(req), req.headers['user-agent'] || null, method
    );
}

function generateTempPassword(){
  let pw = '';
  const bytes = crypto.randomBytes(10);
  for (let i = 0; i < 10; i++){
    pw += ACCESS_CODE_ALPHABET[bytes[i] % ACCESS_CODE_ALPHABET.length];
    if (i === 4) pw += '-';
  }
  return pw;
}

// Reads `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, returns the session row if it's
// valid and unexpired, otherwise null. Expired sessions are deleted lazily
// here rather than needing a separate cleanup job — there's no cron in
// this vanilla-Node backend to run one anyway.
function authenticate(req){
  const header = req.headers['authorization'] || '';
  const match = header.match(/^Bearer\s+(.+)$/);
  if (!match) return null;
  const token = match[1].trim();
  const session = db.prepare('SELECT * FROM sessions WHERE token = ?').get(token);
  if (!session) return null;
  if (new Date(session.expiresAt).getTime() < Date.now()){
    db.prepare('DELETE FROM sessions WHERE token = ?').run(token);
    return null;
  }
  return session;
}

// The one check every account-scoped route makes before touching the
// database. Sends the 401 itself (matching this file's existing
// return-false-and-the-caller-returns convention) so every call site is
// one line: `if (!requireAccount(req, res, accountId)) return;`
function requireAccount(req, res, accountId){
  const session = authenticate(req);
  if (!session || session.accountId !== accountId){
    sendJson(res, 401, { error: 'unauthorized — a valid session token for this account is required' });
    return false;
  }
  return true;
}

// Round 132bf (2026-08-13) — plain regex HTML text extraction for the real
// website-scan endpoint below. No headless browser / JS execution, so a
// single-page app that renders its real content client-side may come back
// thin — that's a genuine limitation of a lightweight fetch, disclosed to
// the caller via whatever it actually finds (an empty excerpt is possible
// and honest, never padded with invented text).
const HTML_NAMED_ENTITIES = {
  nbsp: ' ', amp: '&', quot: '"', apos: "'", lt: '<', gt: '>',
  mdash: '—', ndash: '–', hellip: '…', trade: '™',
  rsquo: '’', lsquo: '‘', rdquo: '”', ldquo: '“', copy: '©', reg: '®'
};
function stripTags(html){
  return html.replace(/<(script|style)[^>]*>[\s\S]*?<\/\1>/gi, ' ')
             .replace(/<[^>]+>/g, ' ')
             .replace(/&#x([0-9a-f]+);/gi, (_, hex) => String.fromCodePoint(parseInt(hex, 16)))
             .replace(/&#(\d+);/g, (_, dec) => String.fromCodePoint(parseInt(dec, 10)))
             .replace(/&([a-z]+);/gi, (m, name) => HTML_NAMED_ENTITIES[name.toLowerCase()] || m)
             .replace(/\s+/g, ' ')
             .trim();
}

// Round 60 — every account created before this round (accessCodeHash IS
// NULL) — including the seeded demo account, CXM-NAT-2026-483, and any
// test data already in cxmedia.db — has no access code, since none was
// ever generated for it. Rather than silently minting and logging one
// server-side (which would mean anyone with shell/log access effectively
// still gets a bypass — the same class of gap this round is closing), this
// is handled by a separate, explicit, one-time endpoint —
// POST /api/auth/claim, below — kept deliberately apart from
// POST /api/auth/login so it's unambiguous in the code and in any request
// log which one actually happened. New accounts (created after this round,
// via POST /api/accounts) never need /claim: their access code is
// generated and returned once at creation time, same as a real signup
// flow. See cxmedia-account-mgmt-scoping.md's password-reset item for the
// real equivalent /claim stands in for.

const ADMIN_API_TOKEN = process.env.ADMIN_API_TOKEN || '';

// Round 59 — HubSpot sync (Deliverable 13). Dormant by design until a real
// HubSpot account exists: reads its access token from an env var rather
// than anything hardcoded, so standing this up later is a config change,
// not a code change.
//
// One-time setup once a HubSpot account exists (full walkthrough also in
// cxmedia-crm-integration-notes.md). Two credential types both work here —
// this code only cares that it gets a Bearer token good for
// crm.objects.contacts.read/write; either path below produces one:
//   Option A — Private App (GA, battle-tested, the original round-59 path):
//     1. HubSpot → Settings → Integrations → Private Apps → create one.
//        Scopes: crm.objects.contacts.read, crm.objects.contacts.write.
//     2. Start this server with HUBSPOT_ACCESS_TOKEN set to that app's
//        access token, e.g.: HUBSPOT_ACCESS_TOKEN=pat-xxxx node backend/server.js
//   Option B — Service Key (round 92 note: HubSpot's newer, purpose-built
//     credential for exactly this "single account, data-only, no webhooks"
//     case — public BETA as of Feb 2026, not GA yet). Create one under the
//     Developer Platform's account-level Service Keys flow with the same
//     two contacts scopes, then set HUBSPOT_ACCESS_TOKEN to its token the
//     same way as Option A — same env var, same Bearer-auth call below,
//     no code difference either way.
//   Whichever is used: that's it — syncLeadToHubspot() below activates
//   automatically on the next lead submitted through assessment.html. No
//   restart-order dependency, no other config.
//
// Round 92 — optional HUBSPOT_PORTAL_ID env var (the numeric Hub ID from
// HubSpot's own account settings/URLs — not a secret, not a credential).
// Set alongside HUBSPOT_ACCESS_TOKEN to have a synced lead's response
// include a direct hubspotContactUrl link back into the HubSpot UI —
// purely a verification convenience; sync itself works without it.
//
// Only HubSpot's standard/default Contact properties (email, firstname,
// lastname, phone, company) are sent by default. HubSpot 400s the whole
// upsert if a property name doesn't exist on the portal yet, and a brand
// new account won't have Verilume-specific fields (quadrant, scores,
// maturity levels, function-group ratings, etc.) defined — so those stay
// off until HUBSPOT_SEND_CUSTOM_PROPERTIES=true is also set, which should
// only happen after creating matching custom Contact properties in
// HubSpot (Settings → Properties → Contact properties) with the exact
// internal names used below. See cxmedia-hubspot-field-spec.md for that
// exact list, ready to paste in.
const HUBSPOT_ACCESS_TOKEN = process.env.HUBSPOT_ACCESS_TOKEN || '';
const HUBSPOT_SEND_CUSTOM_PROPERTIES = process.env.HUBSPOT_SEND_CUSTOM_PROPERTIES === 'true';
// HUBSPOT_PORTAL_ID (round 92) — the numeric Hub ID shown in HubSpot's own
// URLs/account settings (e.g. https://app.hubspot.com/contacts/<portalId>/...).
// Not a credential — it's not secret, it's just an identifier — and it's
// never used to authenticate anything; HUBSPOT_ACCESS_TOKEN alone does
// that. Its only job is letting this server build a direct "open in
// HubSpot" link back to the synced contact record, purely a convenience
// for whoever's verifying a sync worked. Sync still works fully without
// this set; it only changes whether hubspotContactUrl is populated below.
const HUBSPOT_PORTAL_ID = process.env.HUBSPOT_PORTAL_ID || '';

function hubspotContactUrl(contactId){
  if (!HUBSPOT_PORTAL_ID || !contactId) return null;
  return `https://app.hubspot.com/contacts/${encodeURIComponent(HUBSPOT_PORTAL_ID)}/contact/${encodeURIComponent(contactId)}`;
}

// Round 126 (2026-08-08), per direct instruction ("I want the assessment
// selections to flow into HubSpot with our single categorization used on
// the assessment"): every custom property below reuses a categorization
// that already exists as a labeled taxonomy somewhere in assessment.html,
// rather than inventing new terms for CRM purposes —
//   - the 5 Marketing Loop funnel stages (RUBRIC's stage names: Awareness,
//     Consideration, Purchase, Loyalty, Advocacy) for both the Loop's
//     self-rated effectiveness AND the post-Assess measured stage scores;
//   - the 6 marketing function groups (FUNCTION_GROUPS' keys: paid, owned,
//     crm, pr, pos, analytics) for the Channels step's self-ratings;
//   - the 4 named levels per maturity ladder (MAT_LEVELS.media/cx/team/
//     voice — sent as the level's exact name string, e.g. "Last-Touch
//     Attribution", not a bare 1-4 number, so a HubSpot list view reads
//     the same language the visitor actually saw).
// See cxmedia-hubspot-field-spec.md for the full property-by-property
// spec (internal name, label, type, field type, options) to paste into
// HubSpot's Contact properties settings before setting
// HUBSPOT_SEND_CUSTOM_PROPERTIES=true.
const LOOP_STAGE_KEYS = ['Awareness', 'Consideration', 'Purchase', 'Loyalty', 'Advocacy'];
const FUNCTION_GROUP_KEYS = { paid: 'paid_media', owned: 'owned_content_seo', crm: 'crm_lifecycle', pr: 'pr_earned', pos: 'pos_team', analytics: 'analytics_measurement' };

// Builds the full custom-property object from whatever fields are present
// on `lead` — POST /api/leads (registration time) and POST
// /api/leads/enhance (post-Assess) both funnel through this, so a field
// only ever has ONE mapping to a HubSpot property name no matter which
// endpoint populated it.
function buildHubspotCustomProperties(lead){
  const props = {
    verilume_footprint: lead.footprint || '',
    verilume_buyer_type: lead.buyerType || '',
    verilume_industry: lead.industry || '',
    verilume_address: lead.address || '',
    verilume_org_style: lead.orgStyle || '',
    verilume_quadrant: lead.quadrant || '',
    verilume_quadrant_basis: lead.quadrantBasis || '', // 'Estimate' | 'Measured'
    verilume_media_pct: lead.mediaPct != null ? String(lead.mediaPct) : '',
    verilume_cx_pct: lead.cxPct != null ? String(lead.cxPct) : '',
    verilume_media_science_level: lead.mediaScienceLevelName || '',
    verilume_cx_maturity_level: lead.cxMaturityLevelName || '',
    verilume_team_maturity_level: lead.teamMaturityLevelName || '',
    verilume_voice_maturity_level: lead.voiceMaturityLevelName || '',
    verilume_industry_revenue: lead.industryRevenue != null ? String(lead.industryRevenue) : '',
    verilume_market_size: lead.marketSize != null ? String(lead.marketSize) : '',
    verilume_generations: Array.isArray(lead.generations) ? lead.generations.join(';') : '',
    verilume_wealth_tier: Array.isArray(lead.wealthTier) ? lead.wealthTier.join(';') : '',
    verilume_assessed_stages: Array.isArray(lead.assessedStages) ? lead.assessedStages.join(';') : '',
    verilume_engagement_model: lead.engagementModel || ''
  };
  // Loop stage effectiveness (self-rated, 1-4) — one property per RUBRIC stage.
  if (lead.loopEffectiveness){
    LOOP_STAGE_KEYS.forEach(stage => {
      const v = lead.loopEffectiveness[stage];
      props[`verilume_loop_${stage.toLowerCase()}_effectiveness`] = v != null ? String(v) : '';
    });
  }
  // Function-group self-ratings (1-4) — one property per FUNCTION_GROUPS key.
  if (lead.functionGroupRatings){
    Object.entries(FUNCTION_GROUP_KEYS).forEach(([key, propSuffix]) => {
      const v = lead.functionGroupRatings[key];
      props[`verilume_fn_${propSuffix}_rating`] = v != null ? String(v) : '';
    });
  }
  // Measured stage-by-stage scores (0-100%, post-Assess) — one property per RUBRIC stage.
  if (lead.measuredStagePct){
    LOOP_STAGE_KEYS.forEach(stage => {
      const v = lead.measuredStagePct[stage];
      props[`verilume_measured_${stage.toLowerCase()}_pct`] = v != null ? String(v) : '';
    });
  }
  return props;
}

async function syncLeadToHubspot(lead){
  if (!HUBSPOT_ACCESS_TOKEN){
    return { hubspotStatus: 'not_configured' };
  }
  const properties = {
    email: lead.email,
    firstname: lead.firstName || '',
    lastname: lead.lastName || '',
    phone: lead.phone ? `${lead.phoneCountry || ''} ${lead.phone}`.trim() : '',
    company: lead.company || ''
  };
  if (HUBSPOT_SEND_CUSTOM_PROPERTIES){
    Object.assign(properties, buildHubspotCustomProperties(lead));
  }
  // Batch upsert-by-email — creates the contact if this email is new,
  // updates it in place if it already exists (e.g. a repeat visitor
  // re-running the assessment, or this same lead's post-Assess enhance
  // call arriving later in the same session). One HTTP call either way,
  // no separate lookup-then-create/update round trip.
  try {
    const resp = await fetch('https://api.hubapi.com/crm/v3/objects/contacts/batch/upsert', {
      method: 'POST',
      headers: {
        'Authorization': `Bearer ${HUBSPOT_ACCESS_TOKEN}`,
        'Content-Type': 'application/json'
      },
      body: JSON.stringify({ inputs: [{ idProperty: 'email', id: lead.email, properties }] })
    });
    const data = await resp.json().catch(() => ({}));
    if (!resp.ok){
